### install()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference.rst

Installs pretty printing as the default repr for all objects.

```APIDOC
## install()

### Description
Installs pretty printing as the default repr for all objects.

### Method
install

### Parameters
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional - The console to use.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of the repr.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of strings.

### Returns
None
```

### Install Rich pretty printer in REPL

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/introduction.rst

Installs Rich's pretty printer in the REPL so Python data structures are automatically pretty printed with syntax highlighting. After installation, the example list is displayed with Rich formatting.

```python
>>> from rich import pretty
>>> pretty.install()
>>> ["Rich and pretty", True]
```

### Justify alignment example with print

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Demonstrates the difference between default, left, center, and right justification using the justify argument. The example sets a background color to show padding differences.

```default
from rich.console import Console

console = Console(width=20)

style = "bold white on blue"
console.print("Rich", style=style)
console.print("Rich", style=style, justify="left")
console.print("Rich", style=style, justify="center")
console.print("Rich", style=style, justify="right")
```

### Install Rich traceback handler

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/traceback.rst

Install Rich as the default traceback handler so all uncaught exceptions are rendered with highlighting. See install() for configuration options.

```default
from rich.traceback import install
install(show_locals=True)
```

Adds indent guide lines to text.

### Method
with_indent_guides

### Parameters
- **indent_size** (Optional[int]) - Optional - Size of indentation, or None to auto detect. Defaults to None.
- **character** (str, optional) - Optional - Character to use for indentation. Defaults to "│".

### Returns

```

### Show progress while reading a file with rich.progress.open

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use rich.progress.open to get a context manager that displays a progress bar while reading a file. Example reads a JSON file and prints the loaded data.

```python
import json
import rich.progress

with rich.progress.open("data.json", "rb") as file:
    data = json.load(file)
print(data)
```

### Use custom Console with Progress constructor

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Pass an existing Console object to the Progress constructor to use it for both progress display and console output. Example prints a message before starting work.

```python
from my_project import my_console

with Progress(console=my_console) as progress:
    my_console.print("[bold blue]Starting work!")
    do_work(progress)
```

--------------------------------

### rich.traceback.install

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/traceback.rst

Function to install a rich traceback handler globally.

```APIDOC
## rich.traceback.install(console=None, width=100, code_width=88, extra_lines=3, theme=None, word_wrap=False, show_locals=False, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, locals_max_depth=None, locals_hide_dunder=True, locals_hide_sunder=None, locals_overflow=None, indent_guides=True, suppress=(), max_frames=100)

### Description
Installs a rich traceback handler. Once installed, any tracebacks will be printed with syntax highlighting and rich formatting.

### Parameters
- **console** (Optional[Console], optional) - Optional - Console to write exception to. Default uses internal Console instance.
- **width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Width (in characters) of traceback. Defaults to 100.
- **code_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Code width (in characters) of traceback. Defaults to 88.
- **extra_lines** (int, optional) - Optional - Extra lines of code. Defaults to 3.
- **theme** (Optional[str], optional) - Optional - Pygments theme to use in traceback. Defaults to `None` which will pick a theme appropriate for the platform.
- **word_wrap** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable word wrapping of long lines. Defaults to False.
- **show_locals** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable display of local variables. Defaults to False.
- **locals_max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to 10.
- **locals_max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to 80.
- **locals_max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depths of locals before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **locals_hide_dunder** (bool, optional) - Optional - Hide locals prefixed with double underscore. Defaults to True.
- **locals_hide_sunder** (bool, optional) - Optional - Hide locals prefixed with single underscore. Defaults to False.
- **locals_overflow** (OverflowMethod, optional) - Optional - How to handle overflowing locals, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable indent guides in code and locals. Defaults to True.
- **suppress** (Sequence[Union[str, ModuleType]]) - Optional - Optional sequence of modules or paths to exclude from traceback.
- **max_frames** (int) - Optional - Maximum number of frames to show in a traceback, 0 for no maximum. Defaults to 100.

### Returns
The previous exception handler that was replaced.

### Return Type
Callable
```

### Assemble Text with styled parts using assemble

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Combines strings and style pairs into a Text instance using the assemble method. This example is equivalent to the previous ANSI example, producing a Text with 'Hello' in bold magenta and ', World!' plain.

```default
text = Text.assemble(("Hello", "bold magenta"), ", World!")
console.print(text)
```

### Console.log() example with output

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Example of Console.log() output. Logging adds a timestamp column on the left and the file and line of the call on the right. The output shows the log message with timestamp and source location.

```default
>>> console.log("Hello, World!")
```

```default
[16:32:08] Hello, World!                                         <stdin>:1
```

### Console.print() examples

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Examples of using Console.print() to output rich content. Rich converts objects to strings via __str__, performs syntax highlighting, pretty-prints containers, and renders Console Markup in strings.

```default
console.print([1, 2, 3])
console.print("[blue underline]Looks like a link")
console.print(locals())
console.print("FOO", style="white on blue")
```

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/panel.rst

```python
from rich import print
from rich.panel import Panel
print(Panel("Hello, [red]World!"))
```

### rich.pretty.install

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

Install automatic pretty printing in the Python REPL. This function configures the console to use pretty printing for all output.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.install

### Description
Install automatic pretty printing in the Python REPL.

### Parameters
- **console** (Console, optional) - Console instance or None to use global console. Defaults to None.
- **overflow** (Optional[OverflowMethod], optional) - Overflow method. Defaults to "ignore".
- **crop** (Optional[bool], optional) - Enable cropping of long lines. Defaults to False.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Enable indentation guides. Defaults to False.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Maximum depth of nested data structures, or None for no maximum. Defaults to None.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Expand all containers. Defaults to False.
- **max_frames** (int) - Maximum number of frames to show in a traceback, 0 for no maximum. Defaults to 100.

### Returns
- **None** - This function does not return a value.
```

### Auto-generate rich repr with @rich.repr.auto

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

Use the @rich.repr.auto decorator to automatically generate a rich repr for a class whose __init__ parameters match attribute names. The decorator also creates a __repr__, so you get an auto-generated repr even without Rich printing. This example also prints a dictionary of Bird instances with rich.print.

```python
import rich.repr

@rich.repr.auto
class Bird:
    def __init__(self, name, eats=None, fly=True, extinct=False):
        self.name = name
        self.eats = list(eats) if eats else []
        self.fly = fly
        self.extinct = extinct


BIRDS = {
    "gull": Bird("gull", eats=["fish", "chips", "ice cream", "sausage rolls"]),
    "penguin": Bird("penguin", eats=["fish"], fly=False),
    "dodo": Bird("dodo", eats=["fruit"], fly=False, extinct=True)
}
from rich import print
print(BIRDS)
```

### Basic Columns example: clone of ls command

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/columns.rst

Constructs a Columns instance from a directory listing and prints it, using equal=True and expand=True to space the columns evenly. Requires a directory argument on the command line; otherwise prints usage.

```python
import os
import sys

from rich import print
from rich.columns import Columns

if len(sys.argv) < 2:
    print("Usage: python columns.py DIRECTORY")
else:
    directory = os.listdir(sys.argv[1])
    columns = Columns(directory, equal=True, expand=True)
    print(columns)
```

--------------------------------

### Syntax highlighting with Syntax object

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/syntax.rst

Construct a Syntax object from a string and print it to the console. The example reads a Python file and highlights it as Python.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.syntax import Syntax

console = Console()
with open("syntax.py", "rt") as code_file:
    syntax = Syntax(code_file.read(), "python")
console.print(syntax)
```

### Live(renderable=None, console=None, screen=False, auto_refresh=True, refresh_per_second=4, transient=False, redirect_stdout=True, redirect_stderr=True, vertical_overflow='ellipsis', get_renderable=None)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/live.rst

Creates a Live instance to render an auto-updating live display of a given renderable. Configure console, screen mode, refresh rate, and output redirection.

```APIDOC
## Live(renderable=None, console=None, screen=False, auto_refresh=True, refresh_per_second=4, transient=False, redirect_stdout=True, redirect_stderr=True, vertical_overflow='ellipsis', get_renderable=None)

### Description
Creates a Live instance to render an auto-updating live display of a given renderable. Configure console, screen mode, refresh rate, and output redirection.

### Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType, optional) - Optional - The renderable to live display. Defaults to displaying nothing.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional - Optional Console instance. Defaults to an internal Console instance writing to stdout.
- **screen** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable alternate screen mode. Defaults to False.
- **auto_refresh** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable auto refresh. If disabled, you will need to call refresh() or update() with refresh flag. Defaults to True.
- **refresh_per_second** (float, optional) - Optional - Number of times per second to refresh the live display. Defaults to 4.
- **transient** (bool, optional) - Optional - Clear the renderable on exit (has no effect when screen=True). Defaults to False.
- **redirect_stdout** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable redirection of stdout, so `print` may be used. Defaults to True.
- **redirect_stderr** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable redirection of stderr. Defaults to True.
- **vertical_overflow** (VerticalOverflowMethod, optional) - Optional - How to handle renderable when it is too tall for the console. Defaults to "ellipsis".
- **get_renderable** (Callable[[], RenderableType], optional) - Optional - Optional callable to get renderable. Defaults to None.
```

--------------------------------

### Stylize Text with bold magenta

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Creates a Text instance and applies a style to a range of characters using the stylize method, then prints it to the console. The example prints 'Hello, World!' with the first word in bold magenta.

```default
from rich.console import Console
from rich.text import Text

console = Console()
text = Text("Hello, World!")
text.stylize("bold magenta", 0, 6)
console.print(text)
```

### Create a basic live display with Live context manager

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Construct a Live object with a renderable and use it as a context manager. The live display persists for the duration of the context. Update the renderable internally to update the display. The refresh_per_second parameter controls the update rate.

```default
import time

from rich.live import Live
from rich.table import Table

table = Table()
table.add_column("Row ID")
table.add_column("Description")
table.add_column("Level")

with Live(table, refresh_per_second=4):  # update 4 times a second to feel fluid
    for row in range(12):
        time.sleep(0.4)  # arbitrary delay
        # update the renderable internally
        table.add_row(f"{row}", f"description {row}", "[red]ERROR")
```

### Use a custom Console with Live

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Pass a custom Console object to the Live constructor to use it for printing. The example prints a message to the custom console inside the Live context.

```python
from my_project import my_console

with Live(console=my_console) as live:
    my_console.print("[bold blue]Starting work!")
    ...
```

--------------------------------

### Set up RichHandler for logging

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/logging.rst

Sets up a RichHandler as the logging handler for Python's logging module, formatting and colorizing log output. The example configures basicConfig with the handler and logs a simple message.

```python
import logging
from rich.logging import RichHandler

FORMAT = "%(message)s"
logging.basicConfig(
    level="NOTSET", format=FORMAT, datefmt="[%X]", handlers=[RichHandler()]
)

log = logging.getLogger("rich")
log.info("Hello, World!")
```

### Using console.pager() with make_test_card

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Shows how to use the pager context manager to display content in a system pager. Requires importing Console and make_test_card from rich.__main__. The example prints a test card inside the pager context.

```pycon
>>> from rich.console import Console
>>> from rich.__main__ import make_test_card
>>> console = Console()
>>> with console.pager():
        console.print(make_test_card())
```

### Defining and using a custom Theme

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/style.rst

Demonstrates creating a Theme instance and passing it to the Console constructor to use semantic style names.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.theme import Theme
custom_theme = Theme({
    "info": "dim cyan",
    "warning": "magenta",
    "danger": "bold red"
})
console = Console(theme=custom_theme)
console.print("This is information", style="info")
console.print("[warning]The pod bay doors are locked[/warning]")
console.print("Something terrible happened!", style="danger")
```

MARKDOWN = """
# This is an h1

Rich can do a pretty *decent* job of rendering markdown.

1. This is a list item
2. This is another list item
"""

console = Console()

### Syntax highlight code with rich.syntax.Syntax

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Apply syntax highlighting to a code string using rich.syntax.Syntax. The example uses the monokai theme and enables line numbers, then prints the highlighted code to the console.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.syntax import Syntax

my_code = '''
def iter_first_last(values: Iterable[T]) -> Iterable[Tuple[bool, bool, T]]:
    """Iterate and generate a tuple with a flag for first and last value."""
    iter_values = iter(values)
    try:
        previous_value = next(iter_values)
    except StopIteration:
        return
    first = True
    for value in iter_values:
        yield first, False, previous_value
        first = False
        previous_value = value
    yield first, True, previous_value
'''
syntax = Syntax(my_code, "python", theme="monokai", line_numbers=True)
console = Console()
console.print(syntax)
```

### Print/log above progress with progress.console

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use Progress() to create a console accessible via progress.console to print output above the progress display. Example adds a task and iterates to print job messages and advance the progress.

```python
with Progress() as progress:
    task = progress.add_task("twiddling thumbs", total=10)
    for job in range(10):
        progress.console.print(f"Working on job #{job}")
        run_job(job)
        progress.advance(task)
```

### Print to live.console in a Live context

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Use the internal console of a Live instance to print messages above the live display. The example updates a table 4 times per second and prints progress messages via live.console.print.

```python
import time

from rich.live import Live
from rich.table import Table

table = Table()
table.add_column("Row ID")
table.add_column("Description")
table.add_column("Level")

with Live(table, refresh_per_second=4) as live:  # update 4 times a second to feel fluid
    for row in range(12):
        live.console.print(f"Working on row #{row}")
        time.sleep(0.4)
        table.add_row(f"{row}", f"description {row}", "[red]ERROR")
```

### Display content on alternate screen with console.screen()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Use the screen context manager to display content on the alternate screen. The alternate screen is separate from the regular terminal and leaves your command history intact. The example prints a dictionary and returns to the command prompt after 5 seconds.

```python
from time import sleep
from rich.console import Console

console = Console()
with console.screen():
    console.print(locals())
    sleep(5)
```

--------------------------------

### Progress without context manager with start/stop

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

When not using the context manager, call start() before adding tasks and stop() in a finally block to ensure proper cleanup. Uses try/finally to guarantee stop() is called.

```python
import time

from rich.progress import Progress

progress = Progress()
progress.start()
try:
    task1 = progress.add_task("[red]Downloading...", total=1000)
    task2 = progress.add_task("[green]Processing...", total=1000)
    task3 = progress.add_task("[cyan]Cooking...", total=1000)

    while not progress.finished:
        progress.update(task1, advance=0.5)
        progress.update(task2, advance=0.3)
        progress.update(task3, advance=0.9)
        time.sleep(0.02)
finally:
    progress.stop()
```

### open(file, mode, buffering=-1, encoding=None, errors=None, newline=None, total=None, task_id=None, description='Reading...')

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Tracks progress while reading from a binary or text file. Returns a file-like object.

```APIDOC
## open(file, mode, buffering=-1, encoding=None, errors=None, newline=None, total=None, task_id=None, description='Reading...')

### Description
Track progress while reading from a binary file.

### Method
open

### Parameters
- **file** (str | PathLike[str] | bytes) - Required - The path to the file to read.
- **mode** (Literal['rb'] or Literal['r', 'rt']) - Required - The mode to use to open the file. Only supports "r", "rb" or "rt".
- **buffering** (int) - Optional - The buffering strategy to use, see io.open().
- **encoding** (str, optional) - The encoding to use when reading in text mode, see io.open().
- **errors** (str, optional) - The error handling strategy for decoding errors, see io.open().
- **newline** (str, optional) - The strategy for handling newlines in text mode, see io.open().
- **total** (int, optional) - Total number of bytes to read. If none given, os.stat(path).st_size is used.
- **task_id** (TaskID) - Optional - Task to track. Default is new task.
- **description** (str, optional) - Description of task, if new task is created.

### Returns
BinaryIO or TextIO - A readable file-like object.

### Raises
ValueError - When an invalid mode is given.
```

### Implement __rich_console__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

For more advanced rendering, add a __rich_console__ method that accepts a Console and ConsoleOptions and returns an iterable of renderable objects. Using yield makes it a generator. This example renders a Student as a table.

```default
from dataclasses import dataclass
from rich.console import Console, ConsoleOptions, RenderResult
from rich.table import Table

@dataclass
class Student:
    id: int
    name: str
    age: int
    def __rich_console__(self, console: Console, options: ConsoleOptions) -> RenderResult:
        yield f"[b]Student:[/b] #{self.id}"
        my_table = Table("Attribute", "Value")
        my_table.add_row("name", self.name)
        my_table.add_row("age", str(self.age))
        yield my_table
```

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/panel.rst

The Panel constructor accepts title and subtitle arguments to draw a title on the top and a subtitle on the bottom of the panel. The example prints a panel with title 'Welcome' and subtitle 'Thank you'.

```python
from rich import print

```

### rich.pretty.pprint

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

Function to pretty print an object to the console with configurable options for abbreviation, truncation, depth, and indentation guides.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.pprint(_object, *, console=None, indent_guides=True, max_length=None, max_string=None, max_depth=None, expand_all=False)

### Description
A convenience function for pretty printing an object to the console.

### Parameters
- **_object** (Any) - Required - Object to pretty print.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional - Console instance, or None to use default. Defaults to None.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of strings before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depth for nested data structures, or None for unlimited depth. Defaults to None.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable indentation guides. Defaults to True.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand all containers. Defaults to False.

### Return Type
None
```

### Syntax highlighting with from_path()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/syntax.rst

Use the from_path() alternative constructor to load code from disk and auto-detect the file type. This is a more concise way to highlight a file.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.syntax import Syntax

console = Console()
syntax = Syntax.from_path("syntax.py")
console.print(syntax)
```

### Creating a grid with Table.grid for layout

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/tables.rst

Uses Table.grid to create a layout with columns left and right aligned on one line. The example prints a row with a completion message.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.table import Table

grid = Table.grid(expand=True)
grid.add_column()
grid.add_column(justify="right")
grid.add_row("Raising shields", "[bold magenta]COMPLETED [green]:heavy_check_mark:")

print(grid)
```

### Update the renderable with Live.update()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Change the renderable on-the-fly by calling the update() method. Useful when the information is too dynamic to generate by updating a single renderable. The example generates a new table each iteration and updates the live display.

```default
import random
import time

from rich.live import Live
from rich.table import Table


def generate_table() -> Table:
    """Make a new table."""
    table = Table()
    table.add_column("ID")
    table.add_column("Value")
    table.add_column("Status")

    for row in range(random.randint(2, 6)):
        value = random.random() * 100
        table.add_row(
            f"{row}", f"{value:3.2f}", "[red]ERROR" if value < 50 else "[green]SUCCESS"
        )
    return table


with Live(generate_table(), refresh_per_second=4) as live:
    for _ in range(40):
        time.sleep(0.4)
        live.update(generate_table())
```

### Syntax.__init__

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/syntax.rst

Constructs a Syntax object to render syntax highlighted code. Accepts the code string, a lexer, and various formatting options.

```APIDOC
## Syntax.__init__

### Description
Constructs a Syntax object to render syntax highlighted code.

### Method
Constructor

### Signature
Syntax(code, lexer, theme='monokai', dedent=False, line_numbers=False, start_line=1, line_range=None, highlight_lines=None, code_width=None, tab_size=4, word_wrap=False, background_color=None, indent_guides=False, padding=0)

### Parameters
- **code** (str) - Required - Code to highlight.
- **lexer** (Lexer | str) - Required - Lexer to use (see https://pygments.org/docs/lexers/).
- **theme** (str, optional) - Color theme, aka Pygments style (see https://pygments.org/docs/styles/#getting-a-list-of-available-styles). Defaults to "monokai".
- **dedent** (bool, optional) - Enable stripping of initial whitespace. Defaults to False.
- **line_numbers** (bool, optional) - Enable rendering of line numbers. Defaults to False.
- **start_line** (int, optional) - Starting number for line numbers. Defaults to 1.
- **line_range** (Tuple[int | None, int | None], optional) - If given should be a tuple of the start and end line to render. A value of None in the tuple indicates the range is open in that direction.
- **highlight_lines** (Set[int]) - A set of line numbers to highlight.
- **code_width** (Optional[int]) - Width of code to render (not including line numbers), or None to use all available width.
- **tab_size** (int, optional) - Size of tabs. Defaults to 4.
- **word_wrap** (bool, optional) - Enable word wrapping.
- **background_color** (str, optional) - Optional background color, or None to use theme color. Defaults to None.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Show indent guides. Defaults to False.
- **padding** (PaddingDimensions) - Padding to apply around the syntax. Defaults to 0 (no padding).
```

--------------------------------

### Handle exceptions with rich_tracebacks

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/logging.rst

Configures RichHandler with rich_tracebacks=True to format exceptions using Rich's Traceback class, providing more context. The example logs an exception from a division by zero.

```python
import logging
from rich.logging import RichHandler

logging.basicConfig(
    level="NOTSET",
    format="%(message)s",
    datefmt="[%X]",
    handlers=[RichHandler(rich_tracebacks=True)]
)

log = logging.getLogger("rich")
try:
    print(1 / 0)
except Exception:
    log.exception("unable print!")
```

--------------------------------

### Show progress while reading from a URL with wrap_file

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use rich.progress.wrap_file to wrap an existing file object and display a progress bar. You must set the expected number of bytes. Example reads a URL and prints its content line by line.

```python
from time import sleep
from urllib.request import urlopen

from rich.progress import wrap_file

response = urlopen("https://www.textualize.io")
size = int(response.headers["Content-Length"])

with wrap_file(response, size) as file:
    for line in file:
        print(line.decode("utf-8"), end="")
        sleep(0.1)
```

### Use Text.on method to attach click handler in Textual project

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/text.rst

Applies event handlers by keyword arguments, prefixed with '@', to define handlers. This example attaches a click handler to a Text instance; meta information is optional. Returns self for chaining.

```pycon
>>> from rich.text import Text
>>> text = Text("hello world")
>>> text.on(click="view.toggle('world')")
```

### Yield Segment objects for low-level rendering

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

For complete control over rendering, yield Segment objects consisting of text and an optional Style. This example writes multi-colored text for a MyObject instance.

```default
class MyObject:
    def __rich_console__(self, console: Console, options: ConsoleOptions) -> RenderResult:
        yield Segment("My", Style(color="magenta"))
        yield Segment("Object", Style(color="green"))
        yield Segment("()", Style(color="cyan"))
```

--------------------------------

### Update alternate screen with renderable using console.screen()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Update the alternate screen with a renderable using screen.update(). This allows Rich to crop the contents to fit the screen without scrolling. The example displays a countdown in a panel with a blinking message.

```python
from time import sleep

from rich.console import Console
from rich.align import Align
from rich.text import Text
from rich.panel import Panel

console = Console()

with console.screen(style="bold white on red") as screen:
    for count in range(5, 0, -1):
        text = Align.center(
            Text.from_markup(f"[blink]Don't Panic![/blink]\n{count}", justify="center"),
            vertical="middle",
        )
        screen.update(Panel(text))
        sleep(1)
```

### Custom EmailHighlighter with RegexHighlighter

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/highlighting.rst

Defines a custom highlighter that applies a style to email addresses. The highlights list contains a regex with a named group 'email'. The base_style 'example.' is prefixed to the group name to form the style 'example.email', which is defined in a custom Theme. The highlighter is set on the Console to apply to all printed text.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.highlighter import RegexHighlighter
from rich.theme import Theme

class EmailHighlighter(RegexHighlighter):
    """Apply style to anything that looks like an email."""

    base_style = "example."
    highlights = [r"(?P<email>[\w-]+@([\w-]+\.)+[\w-]+)"]


theme = Theme({"example.email": "bold magenta"})
console = Console(highlighter=EmailHighlighter(), theme=theme)
console.print("Send funds to money@example.org")
```

### open

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Documentation for the open function, including parameters and return type.

```APIDOC
## open(file, mode, buffering=-1, encoding=None, errors=None, newline=None, total=None, description='Reading...', auto_refresh=True, console=None, transient=False, get_time=None, refresh_per_second=10, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', disable=False)

### Description
Opens a file and returns a context manager that provides a TextIO object.

### Parameters
- **file** (str | PathLike[str] | bytes) - The file to open.
- **mode** (Literal['rt', 'r']) - The mode to open the file in.
- **buffering** (int, optional) - Buffering policy. Defaults to -1.
- **encoding** (str | None, optional) - Encoding to use. Defaults to None.
- **errors** (str | None, optional) - Error handling. Defaults to None.
- **newline** (str | None, optional) - Newline control. Defaults to None.
- **total** (int | None, optional) - Total size for progress. Defaults to None.
- **description** (str, optional) - Description for progress. Defaults to 'Reading...'.
- **auto_refresh** (bool, optional) - Auto refresh progress. Defaults to True.
- **console** (Console | None, optional) - Console to use. Defaults to None.
- **transient** (bool, optional) - Transient progress. Defaults to False.
- **get_time** (Callable[[], float] | None, optional) - Time function. Defaults to None.
- **refresh_per_second** (float, optional) - Refresh rate. Defaults to 10.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for progress bar. Defaults to 'bar.back'.
- **complete_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for completed portion. Defaults to 'bar.complete'.
- **finished_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for finished bar. Defaults to 'bar.finished'.
- **pulse_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for pulse. Defaults to 'bar.pulse'.
- **disable** (bool, optional) - Disable progress. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **ContextManager[TextIO]** - A context manager for the opened file.
```

--------------------------------

### Write console output to a file with file=report_file

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Writes console output to a file by passing a file-like object to the file argument. The example opens report.txt for writing and uses console.rule to print a formatted header. When writing to a file, consider setting the width argument to avoid wrapping to the current console width.

```python
import sys
from rich.console import Console
from datetime import datetime

with open("report.txt", "wt") as report_file:
    console = Console(file=report_file)
    console.rule(f"Report Generated {datetime.now().ctime()}")
```

### Implement __rich_measure__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

Supply a __rich_measure__ method to let Rich know how many characters an object will take up when rendering. It accepts a Console and ConsoleOptions and returns a Measurement with minimum and maximum characters. This example returns a minimum of 8 for a chess board and the maximum available width.

```default
class ChessBoard:
    def __rich_measure__(self, console: Console, options: ConsoleOptions) -> Measurement:
        return Measurement(8, options.max_width)
```

### Customize progress display by overriding get_renderables

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Override get_renderables in a Progress subclass to wrap the progress table in a Panel. This example yields a Panel containing the tasks table.

```python
from rich.panel import Panel
from rich.progress import Progress

class MyProgress(Progress):
    def get_renderables(self):
        yield Panel(self.make_tasks_table(self.tasks))
```

### Use group decorator to build group from generator

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/group.rst

Use the group() decorator to build a Group from a generator function that yields renderables. This is equivalent to the previous Group class example but useful when the number of renderables is dynamic or larger.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.console import group
from rich.panel import Panel

@group()
def get_panels():
    yield Panel("Hello", style="on blue")
    yield Panel("World", style="on red")

print(Panel(get_panels()))
```

### Progress with custom table column ratios

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Demonstrates a progress bar where the description column takes one third of the terminal width and the bar takes the remaining two thirds, using table_column with ratio values. Includes a loop that tracks 100 items and prints each step.

```python
from time import sleep

from rich.table import Column
from rich.progress import Progress, BarColumn, TextColumn

text_column = TextColumn("{task.description}", table_column=Column(ratio=1))
bar_column = BarColumn(bar_width=None, table_column=Column(ratio=2))
progress = Progress(text_column, bar_column, expand=True)

with progress:
    for n in progress.track(range(100)):
        progress.print(n)
        sleep(0.1)
```

### Capture console output with capture() and get()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Captures console output using the capture() context manager. Inside the with block, console.print writes to the capture; after exiting, capture.get() returns the string that would have been written to the terminal.

```python
from rich.console import Console
console = Console()
with console.capture() as capture:
    console.print("[bold red]Hello[/] World")
str_output = capture.get()
```

### Generate angular repr with @rich.repr.auto(angular=True)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

Set angular=True on the @rich.repr.auto decorator to generate the angular type of repr instead of the default. The class definition is otherwise identical to the previous example.

```python
@rich.repr.auto(angular=True)
class Bird:
    def __init__(self, name, eats=None, fly=True, extinct=False):
        self.name = name
        self.eats = list(eats) if eats else []
        self.fly = fly
        self.extinct = extinct
```

--------------------------------

### Progress class with context manager

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use Progress as a context manager to automatically start/stop the display. Add tasks with add_task() and update using advance. The loop continues until all tasks are finished.

```python
import time

from rich.progress import Progress

with Progress() as progress:

    task1 = progress.add_task("[red]Downloading...", total=1000)
    task2 = progress.add_task("[green]Processing...", total=1000)
    task3 = progress.add_task("[cyan]Cooking...", total=1000)

    while not progress.finished:
        progress.update(task1, advance=0.5)
        progress.update(task2, advance=0.3)
        progress.update(task3, advance=0.9)
        time.sleep(0.02)
```

### TimeRemainingColumn

Track progress file reading from a binary file.

### Parameters

### Traceback.__init__

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/traceback.rst

Constructs a Traceback renderable from a Trace object or the last exception. Accepts numerous options to control width, code display, local variable handling, and suppression.

```APIDOC
## Traceback.__init__

### Description
Constructs a Traceback renderable from a Trace object or the last exception. Accepts numerous options to control width, code display, local variable handling, and suppression.

### Method
Constructor

### Signature
`Traceback(trace=None, *, width=100, code_width=88, extra_lines=3, theme=None, word_wrap=False, show_locals=False, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, locals_max_depth=None, locals_hide_dunder=True, locals_hide_sunder=False, locals_overlow=None, indent_guides=True, suppress=(), max_frames=100)`

### Parameters
- **trace** (Trace, optional) – A Trace object produced from extract. Defaults to None, which uses the last exception.
- **width** (Optional[int], optional) – Number of characters used to traceback. Defaults to 100.
- **code_width** (Optional[int], optional) – Number of code characters used to traceback. Defaults to 88.
- **extra_lines** (int, optional) – Additional lines of code to render. Defaults to 3.
- **theme** (str, optional) – Override pygments theme used in traceback.
- **word_wrap** (bool, optional) – Enable word wrapping of long lines. Defaults to False.
- **show_locals** (bool, optional) – Enable display of local variables. Defaults to False.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) – Enable indent guides in code and locals. Defaults to True.
- **locals_max_length** (int, optional) – Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to 10.
- **locals_max_string** (int, optional) – Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to 80.
- **locals_max_depth** (int, optional) – Maximum depths of locals before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **locals_hide_dunder** (bool, optional) – Hide locals prefixed with double underscore. Defaults to True.
- **locals_hide_sunder** (bool, optional) – Hide locals prefixed with single underscore. Defaults to False.
- **locals_overflow** (OverflowMethod, optional) – How to handle overflowing locals, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **suppress** (Sequence[Union[str, ModuleType]]) – Optional sequence of modules or paths to exclude from traceback.
- **max_frames** (int) – Maximum number of frames to show in a traceback, 0 for no maximum. Defaults to 100.
- **locals_overlow** (Literal['fold', 'crop', 'ellipsis', 'ignore'] | None) – (Note: appears as a parameter in the signature, but no description provided.)

### Returns
A Console renderable that renders a traceback.

### Return Type
Traceback
```

--------------------------------

### Display a status spinner with Console.status

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Use Console.status to display a spinner animation and message while performing tasks. The animation does not block console usage. The example shows a loop that pops tasks from a list, sleeps one second per task, and logs completion messages.

```python
from time import sleep
from rich.console import Console

console = Console()
tasks = [f"task {n}" for n in range(1, 11)]

with console.status("[bold green]Working on tasks...") as status:
    while tasks:
        task = tasks.pop(0)
        sleep(1)
        console.log(f"{task} complete")
```

--------------------------------

### Using EmailHighlighter as a callable

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/highlighting.rst

Shows how to use a highlighter instance as a callable to highlight text on a granular level. The console is created with the theme but without the highlighter, and the highlighter is applied to the string before printing.

```python
console = Console(theme=theme)
highlight_emails = EmailHighlighter()
console.print(highlight_emails("Send funds to money@example.org"))
```

### Create and print a Star Wars movies table

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/tables.rst

Constructs a Table with columns 'Released', 'Title', and 'Box Office', styled with colors and alignment. Adds four rows of Star Wars movie data and prints the table to the console. Rich calculates column widths and wraps text to fit the terminal.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.table import Table

table = Table(title="Star Wars Movies")

table.add_column("Released", justify="right", style="cyan", no_wrap=True)
table.add_column("Title", style="magenta")
table.add_column("Box Office", justify="right", style="green")

table.add_row("Dec 20, 2019", "Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker", "$952,110,690")
table.add_row("May 25, 2018", "Solo: A Star Wars Story", "$393,151,347")
table.add_row("Dec 15, 2017", "Star Wars Ep. V111: The Last Jedi", "$1,332,539,889")
table.add_row("Dec 16, 2016", "Rogue One: A Star Wars Story", "$1,332,439,889")

console = Console()
console.print(table)
```

```console
                           Star Wars Movies                           
┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓
┃     Released ┃ Title                             ┃     Box Office ┃
┡━━━━━━━━━━━━━━╇━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━╇━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┩
│ Dec 20, 2019 │ Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker  │   $952,110,690 │
│ May 25, 2018 │ Solo: A Star Wars Story           │   $393,151,347 │
│ Dec 15, 2017 │ Star Wars Ep. V111: The Last Jedi │ $1,332,539,889 │
│ Dec 16, 2016 │ Rogue One: A Star Wars Story      │ $1,332,439,889 │
└──────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┴────────────────┘
```

### Table(*headers, title=None, caption=None, width=None, min_width=None, box=Box(...), safe_box=None, padding=(0, 1), collapse_padding=False, pad_edge=True, expand=False, show_header=True, show_footer=False, show_edge=True, show_lines=False, leading=0, style='none', row_styles=None, header_style='table.header', footer_style='table.footer', border_style=None, title_style=None, caption_style=None, title_justify='center', caption_justify='center', highlight=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Constructor for the Table class, which creates a console renderable to draw a table with configurable headers, styling, and layout.

```APIDOC
## Table(*headers, title=None, caption=None, width=None, min_width=None, box=Box(...), safe_box=None, padding=(0, 1), collapse_padding=False, pad_edge=True, expand=False, show_header=True, show_footer=False, show_edge=True, show_lines=False, leading=0, style='none', row_styles=None, header_style='table.header', footer_style='table.footer', border_style=None, title_style=None, caption_style=None, title_justify='center', caption_justify='center', highlight=False)

### Description
Creates a console renderable that draws a table. This constructor initializes a table with optional headers, title, caption, and various styling and layout options.

### Method
Constructor

### Parameters
- **\*headers** (Union[Column, str]) - Optional - Column headers, either as a string or a Column instance.
- **title** (Union[str, Text], optional) - Optional - The title of the table rendered at the top. Defaults to None.
- **caption** (Union[str, Text], optional) - Optional - The table caption rendered below. Defaults to None.
- **width** (int, optional) - Optional - The width in characters of the table, or None to automatically fit. Defaults to None.
- **min_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - The minimum width of the table, or None for no minimum. Defaults to None.
- **box** (box.Box, optional) - Optional - One of the constants in box.py used to draw the edges, or None for no box lines. Defaults to box.HEAVY_HEAD.
- **safe_box** (Optional[bool], optional) - Optional - Disable box characters that don't display on windows legacy terminal with raster fonts. Defaults to True.
- **padding** (PaddingDimensions, optional) - Optional - Padding for cells (top, right, bottom, left). Defaults to (0, 1).
- **collapse_padding** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable collapsing of padding around cells. Defaults to False.
- **pad_edge** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable padding of edge cells. Defaults to True.
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand the table to fit the available space if True, otherwise the table width will be auto-calculated. Defaults to False.
- **show_header** (bool, optional) - Optional - Show a header row. Defaults to True.
- **show_footer** (bool, optional) - Optional - Show a footer row. Defaults to False.
- **show_edge** (bool, optional) - Optional - Draw a box around the outside of the table. Defaults to True.
- **show_lines** (bool, optional) - Optional - Draw lines between every row. Defaults to False.
- **leading** (int, optional) - Optional - Number of blank lines between rows (precludes show_lines). Defaults to 0.
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Default style for the table. Defaults to "none".
- **row_styles** (List[Union, str], optional) - Optional - Optional list of row styles, if more than one style is given then the styles will alternate. Defaults to None.
- **header_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the header. Defaults to "table.header".
- **footer_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the footer. Defaults to "table.footer".
- **border_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the border. Defaults to None.
- **title_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the title. Defaults to None.
- **caption_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the caption. Defaults to None.
- **title_justify** (str, optional) - Optional - Justify method for title. Defaults to "center".
- **caption_justify** (str, optional) - Optional - Justify method for caption. Defaults to "center".
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Optional - Highlight cell contents (if str). Defaults to False.

### Returns
A Table instance that can be rendered as a console renderable.
```

### ConsoleOptions

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Documentation for the ConsoleOptions class, including its constructor, properties, and methods.

```APIDOC
## ConsoleOptions

### Description
Options for the `__rich_console__` method, controlling how renderables are displayed.

### Constructor
`ConsoleOptions(size, legacy_windows, min_width, max_width, is_terminal, encoding, max_height, justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=False, highlight=None, markup=None, height=None)`

### Parameters
- **size** (ConsoleDimensions) - Required - Size of console.
- **legacy_windows** (bool) - Required - Flag for legacy windows.
- **min_width** (int) - Required - Minimum width of renderable.
- **max_width** (int) - Required - Maximum width of renderable.
- **is_terminal** (bool) - Required - True if the target is a terminal, otherwise False.
- **encoding** (str) - Required - Encoding of terminal.
- **max_height** (int) - Required - Height of container (starts as terminal).
- **justify** (Literal['default', 'left', 'center', 'right', 'full'] | None) - Optional - Justify value override for renderable.
- **overflow** (Literal['fold', 'crop', 'ellipsis', 'ignore'] | None) - Optional - Overflow value override for renderable.
- **no_wrap** (bool | None) - Optional - Disable wrapping for text.
- **highlight** (bool | None) - Optional - Highlight override for render_str.
- **markup** (bool | None) - Optional - Enable markup when rendering strings.
- **height** (int | None) - Optional - Height of the renderable.

### Properties
- **ascii_only** (bool) - Check if renderables should use ascii only.
- **encoding** (str) - Encoding of terminal.
- **highlight** (bool | None) - Highlight override for render_str.
- **is_terminal** (bool) - True if the target is a terminal, otherwise False.
- **justify** (Literal['default', 'left', 'center', 'right', 'full'] | None) - Justify value override for renderable.
- **legacy_windows** (bool) - Flag for legacy windows.
- **markup** (bool | None) - Enable markup when rendering strings.
- **max_height** (int) - Height of container (starts as terminal).
- **max_width** (int) - Maximum width of renderable.
- **min_width** (int) - Minimum width of renderable.
- **no_wrap** (bool | None) - Disable wrapping for text.
- **overflow** (Literal['fold', 'crop', 'ellipsis', 'ignore'] | None) - Overflow value override for renderable.
- **size** (ConsoleDimensions) - Size of console.

### Methods

#### copy()
Return a copy of the options.

**Returns:**
- ConsoleOptions - A copy of self.

#### reset_height()
Return a copy of the options with height set to `None`.

**Returns:**
- ConsoleOptions - New console options instance.

#### update(*, width=NoChange, min_width=NoChange, max_width=NoChange, justify=NoChange, overflow=NoChange, no_wrap=NoChange, highlight=NoChange, markup=NoChange, height=NoChange)
Update values, return a copy.

**Parameters:**
- **width** (int | NoChange) - Optional - New width.
- **min_width** (int | NoChange) - Optional - New minimum width.
- **max_width** (int | NoChange) - Optional - New maximum width.
- **justify** (Literal['default', 'left', 'center', 'right', 'full'] | None | NoChange) - Optional - New justify value.
- **overflow** (Literal['fold', 'crop', 'ellipsis', 'ignore'] | None | NoChange) - Optional - New overflow value.
- **no_wrap** (bool | None | NoChange) - Optional - New no_wrap value.
- **highlight** (bool | None | NoChange) - Optional - New highlight value.
- **markup** (bool | None | NoChange) - Optional - New markup value.
- **height** (int | None | NoChange) - Optional - New height.

**Returns:**
- ConsoleOptions - New console options instance.

#### update_dimensions(width, height)
Update the width and height, and return a copy.

**Parameters:**
- **width** (int) - Required - New width (sets both min_width and max_width).
- **height** (int) - Required - New height.

**Returns:**
- ConsoleOptions - New console options instance.

#### update_height(height)
Update the height, and return a copy.

**Parameters:**
- **height** (int) - Required - New height.

**Returns:**
- ConsoleOptions - New console options instance.

#### update_width(width)
Update just the width, return a copy.

**Parameters:**
- **width** (int) - Required - New width (sets both min_width and max_width).

**Returns:**
- ConsoleOptions - New console options instance.
```

### rich.progress.open

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Documentation for the rich.progress.open function, which reads bytes from a file while tracking progress.

```APIDOC
## rich.progress.open

### Description
Read bytes from a file while tracking progress. This function opens a file (or accepts a binary file-like object) and returns a context manager that yields a progress reader, displaying a progress bar during the read operation.

### Method
Function call

### Endpoint
`rich.progress.open(file, mode='rb', buffering=-1, encoding=None, errors=None, newline=None, total=None, description='Reading...', auto_refresh=True, console=None, transient=False, get_time=None, refresh_per_second=10, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', disable=False)`

### Parameters
- **file** (str | PathLike[str] | bytes) - Required - The path to the file to read, or a file-like object in binary mode.
- **mode** (Literal['rb']) - Required - The mode to use to open the file. Only supports "r", "rb" or "rt".
- **buffering** (int) - Optional - The buffering strategy to use, see `io.open()`. Defaults to -1.
- **encoding** (str | None) - Optional - The encoding to use when reading in text mode, see `io.open()`. Defaults to None.
- **errors** (str | None) - Optional - The error handling strategy for decoding errors, see `io.open()`. Defaults to None.
- **newline** (str | None) - Optional - The strategy for handling newlines in text mode, see `io.open()`. Defaults to None.
- **total** (int | None) - Optional - Total number of bytes to read. Must be provided if reading from a file handle. Default for a path is os.stat(file).st_size.
- **description** (str) - Optional - Description of task show next to progress bar. Defaults to "Reading".
- **auto_refresh** (bool) - Optional - Automatic refresh, disable to force a refresh after each iteration. Default is True.
- **console** (Console | None) - Optional - Console to write to. Default creates internal Console instance.
- **transient** (bool) - Optional - Clear the progress on exit. Defaults to False.
- **get_time** (Callable[[], float] | None) - Optional - A callable that returns the current time. Defaults to None.
- **refresh_per_second** (float) - Optional - Number of times per second to refresh the progress information. Defaults to 10.
- **style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for the bar background. Defaults to "bar.back".
- **complete_style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for the completed bar. Defaults to "bar.complete".
- **finished_style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for a finished bar. Defaults to "bar.finished".
- **pulse_style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for pulsing bars. Defaults to "bar.pulse".
- **disable** (bool) - Optional - Disable display of progress. Defaults to False.

### Returns
A context manager yielding a progress reader.

### Return Type
ContextManager[BinaryIO]
```

--------------------------------

### Prompt.ask

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/prompt.rst

The Prompt.ask classmethod is a shortcut to construct and run a prompt loop and return the result.

```APIDOC
## Prompt.ask

### Description
Shortcut to construct and run a prompt loop and return the result.

### Method
ask

### Signature
```python
@classmethod
ask(prompt: str | Text = '', console: Console | None = None, password: bool = False, choices: List[str] | None = None, case_sensitive: bool = True, show_default: bool = True, show_choices: bool = True, default: DefaultType, stream: TextIO | None = None) -> DefaultType | PromptType
```

### Parameters
- **prompt** (str | Text) - Optional - Prompt text. Defaults to "".
- **console** (Console | None) - Optional - A Console instance or None to use global console. Defaults to None.
- **password** (bool) - Optional - Enable password input. Defaults to False.
- **choices** (List[str] | None) - Optional - A list of valid choices. Defaults to None.
- **case_sensitive** (bool) - Optional - Matching of choices should be case-sensitive. Defaults to True.
- **show_default** (bool) - Optional - Show default in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **show_choices** (bool) - Optional - Show choices in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **default** (DefaultType) - Required - Default value to use if no input is provided.
- **stream** (TextIO | None) - Optional - Stream to read input from. Defaults to None.

### Returns
- **DefaultType | PromptType** - The result of the prompt loop.

### Example
```pycon
>>> name = Prompt.ask("Enter your name")
```
```

--------------------------------

### Prompt.ask

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/prompt.rst

Prompts the user for input with optional parameters for password, choices, and default values.

```APIDOC
## Prompt.ask

### Description
Prompts the user for input with optional parameters for password, choices, and default values.

### Method
ask

### Parameters
- **prompt** (TextType, optional) - Prompt text. Defaults to "".
- **console** (Console, optional) - A Console instance or None to use global console. Defaults to None.
- **password** (bool, optional) - Enable password input. Defaults to False.
- **choices** (List[str], optional) - A list of valid choices. Defaults to None.
- **case_sensitive** (bool, optional) - Matching of choices should be case-sensitive. Defaults to True.
- **show_default** (bool, optional) - Show default in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **show_choices** (bool, optional) - Show choices in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **stream** (TextIO, optional) - Optional text file open for reading to get input. Defaults to None.

### Example
```pycon

```
```

### ProgressBar

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress_bar.rst

Documentation for the ProgressBar class, including its constructor, properties, and update method.

```APIDOC
## ProgressBar

### Description
Renders a progress bar, used by rich.progress. It can display a determinate progress bar with a total and completed count, or an indeterminate pulsing animation when total is None.

### Constructor
`ProgressBar(total=100.0, completed=0, width=None, pulse=False, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', animation_time=None)`

### Parameters
- **total** (float, optional) - Number of steps in the bar. Defaults to 100. Set to None to render a pulsing animation.
- **completed** (float, optional) - Number of steps completed. Defaults to 0.
- **width** (int, optional) - Width of the bar, or None for maximum width. Defaults to None.
- **pulse** (bool, optional) - Enable pulse effect. Defaults to False. Will pulse if a None total was passed.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the bar background. Defaults to "bar.back".
- **complete_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the completed bar. Defaults to "bar.complete".
- **finished_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for a finished bar. Defaults to "bar.finished".
- **pulse_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for pulsing bars. Defaults to "bar.pulse".
- **animation_time** (Optional[float], optional) - Time in seconds to use for animation, or None to use system time.

### Properties
- **percentage_completed** (float | None) - Calculate percentage complete.

### Methods
#### update(completed, total=None)
Update progress with new values.

**Parameters:**
- **completed** (float) - Number of steps completed.
- **total** (float, optional) - Total number of steps, or None to not change. Defaults to None.

**Return type:** None
```

### Set flexible layout ratio with Layout.ratio

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/layout.rst

Resets the size and sets the ratio of the upper layout to 2, making it take up two thirds of the space because the default ratio is 1.

```python
layout["upper"].size = None
layout["upper"].ratio = 2
print(layout)
```

### Display directory listing in columns with rich.columns.Columns

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

A basic clone of the ls command that displays a directory listing in columns. It takes a directory path as a command-line argument and prints the contents using rich.columns.Columns.

```python
import os
import sys

from rich import print
from rich.columns import Columns

directory = os.listdir(sys.argv[1])
print(Columns(directory))
```

--------------------------------

### Create a table with rich.table.Table

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Creates a table with the Table class, showing header styling, column alignment, and console markup in cells. The Table resizes columns to fit the terminal width, wrapping text as needed.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.table import Table

console = Console()

table = Table(show_header=True, header_style="bold magenta")
table.add_column("Date", style="dim", width=12)
table.add_column("Title")
table.add_column("Production Budget", justify="right")
table.add_column("Box Office", justify="right")
table.add_row(
    "Dec 20, 2019", "Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker", "$275,000,000", "$375,126,118"
)
table.add_row(
    "May 25, 2018",
    "[red]Solo[/red]: A Star Wars Story",
    "$275,000,000",
    "$393,151,347",
)
table.add_row(
    "Dec 15, 2017",
    "Star Wars Ep. VIII: The Last Jedi",
    "$262,000,000",
    "[bold]$1,332,539,889[/bold]",
)

console.print(table)
```

### RichHandler(level=0, console=None, *, show_time=True, omit_repeated_times=True, show_level=True, show_path=True, enable_link_path=True, highlighter=None, markup=False, rich_tracebacks=False, tracebacks_width=None, tracebacks_code_width=88, tracebacks_extra_lines=3, tracebacks_theme=None, tracebacks_word_wrap=True, tracebacks_show_locals=False, tracebacks_suppress=(), tracebacks_max_frames=100, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, log_time_format='[%x %X]', keywords=None)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/logging.rst

Constructs a RichHandler instance. This handler renders log output with Rich, displaying time, level, message, and file in columns. The level is color coded and the message is syntax highlighted.

```APIDOC
## RichHandler(level=0, console=None, *, show_time=True, omit_repeated_times=True, show_level=True, show_path=True, enable_link_path=True, highlighter=None, markup=False, rich_tracebacks=False, tracebacks_width=None, tracebacks_code_width=88, tracebacks_extra_lines=3, tracebacks_theme=None, tracebacks_word_wrap=True, tracebacks_show_locals=False, tracebacks_suppress=(), tracebacks_max_frames=100, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, log_time_format='[%x %X]', keywords=None)

### Description
Constructs a RichHandler instance. This handler renders log output with Rich, displaying time, level, message, and file in columns. The level is color coded and the message is syntax highlighted.

### Parameters
- **level** (Union[int, str], optional) - Log level. Defaults to logging.NOTSET.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional console instance to write logs. Default will use a global console instance writing to stdout.
- **show_time** (bool, optional) - Show a column for the time. Defaults to True.
- **omit_repeated_times** (bool, optional) - Omit repetition of the same time. Defaults to True.
- **show_level** (bool, optional) - Show a column for the level. Defaults to True.
- **show_path** (bool, optional) - Show the path to the original log call. Defaults to True.
- **enable_link_path** (bool, optional) - Enable terminal link of path column to file. Defaults to True.
- **highlighter** (Highlighter, optional) - Highlighter to style log messages, or None to use ReprHighlighter. Defaults to None.
- **markup** (bool, optional) - Enable console markup in log messages. Defaults to False.
- **rich_tracebacks** (bool, optional) - Enable rich tracebacks with syntax highlighting and formatting. Defaults to False.
- **tracebacks_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Number of characters used to render tracebacks, or None for full width. Defaults to None.
- **tracebacks_code_width** (int, optional) - Number of code characters used to render tracebacks, or None for full width. Defaults to 88.
- **tracebacks_extra_lines** (int, optional) - Additional lines of code to render tracebacks, or None for full width. Defaults to None.
- **tracebacks_theme** (str, optional) - Override pygments theme used in traceback.
- **tracebacks_word_wrap** (bool, optional) - Enable word wrapping of long tracebacks lines. Defaults to True.
- **tracebacks_show_locals** (bool, optional) - Enable display of locals in tracebacks. Defaults to False.
- **tracebacks_suppress** (Sequence[Union[str, ModuleType]]) - Optional sequence of modules or paths to exclude from traceback.
- **tracebacks_max_frames** (int, optional) - Optional maximum number of frames returned by traceback.
- **locals_max_length** (int, optional) - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to 10.
- **locals_max_string** (int, optional) - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to 80.
- **log_time_format** (Union[str, TimeFormatterCallable], optional) - If `log_time` is enabled, either string for strftime or callable that formats the time. Defaults to "[%x %X] ".
- **keywords** (List[str], optional) - List of words to highlight instead of `RichHandler.KEYWORDS`.
```

### Combining Style instances

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/style.rst

Shows how to modify an existing style by adding another Style instance to it.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.style import Style
console = Console()

base_style = Style.parse("cyan")
console.print("Hello, World", style = base_style + Style(underline=True))
```

--------------------------------

### Implement __rich__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

Implement a __rich__ method on your class to customize console output. It accepts no arguments and should return an object Rich knows how to render, such as a Text or Table. Returning a plain string renders as Console Markup.

```default
class MyObject:
    def __rich__(self) -> str:
        return "[bold cyan]MyObject()"
```

### Padding(renderable, pad=(0, 0, 0, 0), style='none', expand=True)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/padding.rst

Creates a Padding instance that draws space around the given renderable. The pad parameter can be specified in CSS style with 1, 2, or 4 integers.

```APIDOC
## Padding(renderable, pad=(0, 0, 0, 0), style='none', expand=True)

### Description
Draw space around content. The pad parameter can be specified with 1, 2, or 4 integers (CSS style).

### Constructor Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - Required - String or other renderable.
- **pad** (Union[int, Tuple[int]]) - Optional - Padding for top, right, bottom, and left borders. May be specified with 1, 2, or 4 integers (CSS style). Defaults to (0, 0, 0, 0).
- **style** (Union[str, Style]) - Optional - Style for padding characters. Defaults to "none".
- **expand** (bool) - Optional - Expand padding to fit available width. Defaults to True.

### Example
```pycon
>>> print(Padding("Hello", (2, 4), style="on blue"))
```
```

### Capture output with StringIO for unit tests

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Alternative capturing method by setting the Console file to an io.StringIO. This is recommended for unit tests; after printing, console.file.getvalue() retrieves the output.

```python
from io import StringIO
from rich.console import Console
console = Console(file=StringIO())
console.print("[bold red]Hello[/] World")
str_output = console.file.getvalue()
```

### Syntax.from_path

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/syntax.rst

Classmethod that constructs a Syntax object from a file. The lexer can be auto-detected from the path or file content.

```APIDOC
## Syntax.from_path

### Description
Constructs a Syntax object from a file.

### Method
Classmethod

### Signature
from_path(path, encoding='utf-8', lexer=None, theme='monokai', dedent=False, line_numbers=False, line_range=None, start_line=1, highlight_lines=None, code_width=None, tab_size=4, word_wrap=False, background_color=None, indent_guides=False, padding=0)

### Parameters
- **path** (str) - Required - Path to file to highlight.
- **encoding** (str) - Encoding of file. Defaults to 'utf-8'.
- **lexer** (str | Lexer, optional) - Lexer to use. If None, lexer will be auto-detected from path/file content.
- **theme** (str, optional) - Color theme, aka Pygments style (see https://pygments.org/docs/styles/#getting-a-list-of-available-styles). Defaults to "monokai".
- **dedent** (bool, optional) - Enable stripping of initial whitespace. Defaults to True.
- **line_numbers** (bool, optional) - Enable rendering of line numbers. Defaults to False.
- **start_line** (int, optional) - Starting number for line numbers. Defaults to 1.
- **line_range** (Tuple[int, int], optional) - If given should be a tuple of the start and end line to render.
- **highlight_lines** (Set[int]) - A set of line numbers to highlight.
- **code_width** (int | None) - Width of code to render (not including line numbers), or None to use all available width.
- **tab_size** (int, optional) - Size of tabs. Defaults to 4.
- **word_wrap** (bool, optional) - Enable word wrapping of code.
- **background_color** (str, optional) - Optional background color, or None to use theme color. Defaults to None.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Show indent guides. Defaults to False.
- **padding** (PaddingDimensions) - Padding to apply around the syntax. Defaults to 0 (no padding).

### Returns
- **Syntax** - A Syntax object that may be printed to the console.
```

### Panel(renderable, box=Box(...), title=None, title_align='center', subtitle=None, subtitle_align='center', safe_box=None, expand=True, style='none', border_style='none', width=None, height=None, padding=(0, 1), highlight=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/panel.rst

Creates a console renderable that draws a border around its contents. Used when you want to display content in a styled box with optional title and subtitle.

```APIDOC
## Panel

### Description
A console renderable that draws a border around its contents.

### Method
Constructor (class instantiation)

### Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - Required - A console renderable object.
- **box** (Box) - Optional - A Box instance that defines the look of the border. Defaults to box.ROUNDED.
- **title** (Optional[TextType], optional) - Optional - Optional title displayed in panel header. Defaults to None.
- **title_align** (AlignMethod, optional) - Optional - Alignment of title. Defaults to "center".
- **subtitle** (Optional[TextType], optional) - Optional - Optional subtitle displayed in panel footer. Defaults to None.
- **subtitle_align** (AlignMethod, optional) - Optional - Alignment of subtitle. Defaults to "center".
- **safe_box** (bool, optional) - Optional - Disable box characters that don't display on windows legacy terminal with raster fonts. Defaults to True.
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Optional - If True the panel will stretch to fill the console width, otherwise it will be sized to fit the contents. Defaults to True.
- **style** (str, optional) - Optional - The style of the panel (border and contents). Defaults to "none".
- **border_style** (str, optional) - Optional - The style of the border. Defaults to "none".
- **width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Optional width of panel. Defaults to None to auto-detect.
- **height** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Optional height of panel. Defaults to None to auto-detect.
- **padding** (Optional[PaddingDimensions]) - Optional - Optional padding around renderable. Defaults to 0.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable automatic highlighting of panel title (if str). Defaults to False.

### Example
```pycon
>>> console.print(Panel("Hello, World!"))
```
```

### IntPrompt.ask

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/prompt.rst

IntPrompt is a prompt that returns an integer. Its ask method returns an integer result.

```APIDOC
## IntPrompt.ask

### Description
A prompt that returns an integer. The ask method is inherited from PromptBase and returns an integer result.

### Method
ask

### Signature
```python
@classmethod
ask(prompt: str | Text = '', console: Console | None = None, password: bool = False, choices: List[str] | None = None, case_sensitive: bool = True, show_default: bool = True, show_choices: bool = True, default: DefaultType, stream: TextIO | None = None) -> DefaultType | PromptType
```

### Parameters
- **prompt** (str | Text) - Optional - Prompt text. Defaults to "".
- **console** (Console | None) - Optional - A Console instance or None to use global console. Defaults to None.
- **password** (bool) - Optional - Enable password input. Defaults to False.
- **choices** (List[str] | None) - Optional - A list of valid choices. Defaults to None.
- **case_sensitive** (bool) - Optional - Matching of choices should be case-sensitive. Defaults to True.
- **show_default** (bool) - Optional - Show default in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **show_choices** (bool) - Optional - Show choices in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **default** (DefaultType) - Required - Default value to use if no input is provided.
- **stream** (TextIO | None) - Optional - Stream to read input from. Defaults to None.

### Returns
- **DefaultType | PromptType** - The result of the prompt loop. For IntPrompt, this is an integer.

### Example
```pycon
>>> burrito_count = IntPrompt.ask("How many burritos do you want to order")
```
```

### export_html(, theme=None, clear=True, code_format=None, inline_styles=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Generates HTML from console contents. Requires record=True argument in constructor.

```APIDOC
## export_html(, theme=None, clear=True, code_format=None, inline_styles=False)

### Description
Generates HTML from console contents. Requires record=True argument in constructor.

### Method
export_html

### Parameters
- **theme** (TerminalTheme, optional) - TerminalTheme object containing console colors.
- **clear** (bool, optional) - Clear record buffer after exporting. Defaults to True.
- **code_format** (str, optional) - Format string to render HTML. In addition to '{foreground}', '{background}', and '{code}', should contain '{stylesheet}' if inline_styles is False.
- **inline_styles** (bool, optional) - If True styles will be inlined in to spans, which makes files larger but easier to cut and paste markup. If False, styles will be embedded in a style tag. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **str** - String containing console contents as HTML.
```
