### rich.progress.track

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Function to track progress by iterating over a sequence.

```APIDOC
## rich.progress.track(sequence, description='Working...', total=None, completed=0, auto_refresh=True, console=None, transient=False, get_time=None, refresh_per_second=10, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', update_period=0.1, disable=False, show_speed=True)

### Description
Track progress by iterating over a sequence. You can also track progress of an iterable, which might require that you additionally specify `total`.

### Parameters
- **sequence** (Iterable[ProgressType]) - Required - Values you wish to iterate over and track progress.
- **description** (str, optional) - Description of task show next to progress bar. Defaults to "Working".
- **total** (Optional[float]) - Total number of steps. Default is len(sequence).
- **completed** (int, optional) - Number of steps completed so far. Defaults to 0.
- **auto_refresh** (bool, optional) - Automatic refresh, disable to force a refresh after each iteration. Default is True.
- **transient** (bool) - Clear the progress on exit. Defaults to False.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Console to write to. Default creates internal Console instance.
- **refresh_per_second** (float) - Number of times per second to refresh the progress information. Defaults to 10.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the bar background. Defaults to "bar.back".
- **complete_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the completed bar. Defaults to "bar.complete".
- **finished_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for a finished bar. Defaults to "bar.finished".
- **pulse_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for pulsing bars. Defaults to "bar.pulse".
- **update_period** (float, optional) - Minimum time (in seconds) between calls to update(). Defaults to 0.1.
- **disable** (bool, optional) - Disable display of progress.
- **show_speed** (bool, optional) - Show speed if total isn't known. Defaults to True.
- **get_time** (Optional[Callable[[], float]]) - Optional callable to get the current time.

### Returns
An iterable of the values in the sequence.

### Return Type
Iterable[ProgressType]
```

### rich.pretty.pretty_repr

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

Function to generate a prettified repr string of an object, expanding it to multiple lines to fit within a specified width.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.pretty_repr(_object, *, max_width=80, indent_size=4, max_length=None, max_string=None, max_depth=None, expand_all=False)

### Description
Prettify repr string by expanding on to new lines to fit within a given width.

### Parameters
- **_object** (Any) - Required - Object to repr.
- **max_width** (int, optional) - Optional - Desired maximum width of repr string. Defaults to 80.
- **indent_size** (int, optional) - Optional - Number of spaces to indent. Defaults to 4.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable truncating. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depth of nested data structure, or None for no depth. Defaults to None.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand all containers regardless of available width. Defaults to False.

### Returns
A possibly multi-line representation of the object.

### Return Type
str
```

### Inspect a Python object with Rich

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/introduction.rst

Uses Rich's `inspect()` function to generate a report on a Python object, including its methods. This is a debug aid and shows the output Rich can generate.

```python
>>> from rich import inspect
>>> from rich.color import Color
>>> color = Color.parse("red")
>>> inspect(color, methods=True)
```

--------------------------------

### Suppress framework frames with tracebacks_suppress

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/logging.rst

Suppresses framework frames (e.g., click) from Rich tracebacks by passing tracebacks_suppress=[click] to RichHandler. Suppressed frames show only the line and file, without code.

```python
import click
import logging
from rich.logging import RichHandler

logging.basicConfig(
    level="NOTSET",
    format="%(message)s",
    datefmt="[%X]",
    handlers=[RichHandler(rich_tracebacks=True, tracebacks_suppress=[click])]
)
```

--------------------------------

### Group multiple renderables with Group class

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/group.rst

Use the Group class to combine two Panel renderables into a single renderable, which you can then wrap in another Panel for display. Child renderables are passed as positional arguments.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.console import Group
from rich.panel import Panel

panel_group = Group(
    Panel("Hello", style="on blue"),
    Panel("World", style="on red"),
)
print(Panel(panel_group))
```

### rich.inspect()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/init.rst

Inspects any Python object and displays summarized information, with options to show methods, help, private attributes, and more.

```APIDOC
## rich.inspect(obj, console=None, title=None, help=False, methods=False, docs=True, private=False, dunder=False, sort=True, all=False, value=True)

### Description
Inspect any Python object. Provides summarized info by default, with options to show methods, full help, private attributes, dunder attributes, or all attributes.

### Method
Function

### Signature
`rich.inspect(obj, console=None, title=None, help=False, methods=False, docs=True, private=False, dunder=False, sort=True, all=False, value=True)`

### Parameters
- **obj** (Any) - Required - An object to inspect.
- **title** (str, optional) - Title to display over inspect result, or None use type. Defaults to None.
- **help** (bool, optional) - Show full help text rather than just first paragraph. Defaults to False.
- **methods** (bool, optional) - Enable inspection of callables. Defaults to False.
- **docs** (bool, optional) - Also render doc strings. Defaults to True.
- **private** (bool, optional) - Show private attributes (beginning with underscore). Defaults to False.
- **dunder** (bool, optional) - Show attributes starting with double underscore. Defaults to False.
- **sort** (bool, optional) - Sort attributes alphabetically, callables at the top, leading and trailing underscores ignored. Defaults to True.
- **all** (bool, optional) - Show all attributes. Defaults to False.
- **value** (bool, optional) - Pretty print value. Defaults to True.
- **console** (Console | None) - Optional console to use.

### Returns
- **None**
```

Classmethod

### Check if object is RichRenderable with isinstance

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/abc.rst

if isinstance(my_object, RichRenderable):

```

### Convert ANSI formatted text to Text using from_ansi

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Converts a string with ANSI escape codes into a Text object using the from_ansi method, then prints the spans of the Text. The ANSI code [1;35mHello[0m applies bold magenta to 'Hello'.

```default
text = Text.from_ansi("\033[1;35mHello\033[0m, World!")
console.print(text.spans)
```

--------------------------------

### Print recursive error with max_frames

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/traceback.rst

Print a recursive error traceback with max_frames=20. The max_frames argument defaults to 100; if a traceback exceeds that, only the first 50 and last 50 frames are shown. Set max_frames to 0 to disable this feature.

```default
from rich.console import Console


def foo(n):
    return bar(n)


def bar(n):
    return foo(n)


console = Console()

try:
    foo(1)
except Exception:
    console.print_exception(max_frames=20)
```

--------------------------------

### save_svg

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Method to export console contents to an SVG file.

```APIDOC
## save_svg(path, title='Rich', theme=None, clear=True, code_format='<svg class="rich-terminal" viewBox="0 0 {width} {height}" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">\n    <!-- Generated with Rich https://www.textualize.io -->\n    <style>\n\n    @font-face {{\n        font-family: "Fira Code";\n        src: local("FiraCode-Regular"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff2/FiraCode-Regular.woff2") format("woff2"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff/FiraCode-Regular.woff") format("woff");\n        font-style: normal;\n        font-weight: 400;\n    }}\n    @font-face {{\n        font-family: "Fira Code";\n        src: local("FiraCode-Bold"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff2/FiraCode-Bold.woff2") format("woff2"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff/FiraCode-Bold.woff") format("woff");\n        font-style: bold;\n        font-weight: 700;\n    }}\n\n    .{unique_id}-matrix {{\n        font-family: Fira Code, monospace;\n        font-size: {char_height}px;\n        line-height: {line_height}px;\n        font-variant-east-asian: full-width;\n    }}\n\n    .{unique_id}-title {{\n        font-size: 18px;\n        font-weight: bold;\n        font-family: arial;\n    }}\n\n    {styles}\n    </style>\n\n    <defs>\n    <clipPath id="{unique_id}-clip-terminal">\n      <rect x="0" y="0" width="{terminal_width}" height="{terminal_height}" />\n    </clipPath>\n    {lines}\n    </defs>\n\n    {chrome}\n    <g transform="translate({terminal_x}, {terminal_y})" clip-path="url(#{unique_id}-clip-terminal)">\n    {backgrounds}\n    <g class="{unique_id}-matrix">\n    {matrix}\n    </g>\n    </g>\n</svg>\n', font_aspect_ratio=0.61, unique_id=None)

### Description
Generates an SVG file from the console contents. Requires `record=True` in the Console constructor.

### Method
save_svg

### Parameters
- **path** (Union[str, PathLike[str]]) - Required - The path to write the SVG to.
- **title** (str, optional) - The title of the tab in the output image.
- **theme** (TerminalTheme, optional) - The `TerminalTheme` object to use to style the terminal.
- **clear** (bool, optional) - Clear record buffer after exporting. Defaults to `True`.
- **code_format** (str, optional) - Format string used to generate the SVG. Rich will inject a number of variables into the string in order to form the final SVG output. The default template used and the variables injected by Rich can be found by inspecting the `console.CONSOLE_SVG_FORMAT` variable.
- **font_aspect_ratio** (float, optional) - The width to height ratio of the font used in the `code_format` string. Defaults to 0.61, which is the width to height ratio of Fira Code (the default font). If you aren't specifying a different font inside `code_format`, you probably don't need this.
- **unique_id** (str, optional) - Unique id that is used as the prefix for various elements (CSS styles, node ids). If not set, this defaults to a computed value based on the recorded content.

### Return Type
None
```

### Console.on_broken_pipe

Handles BrokenPipeError events, typically by exiting the app; can be overridden in subclasses.

```APIDOC
## Console.on_broken_pipe()

### Description
This function is called when a BrokenPipeError is raised. This can occur when piping Textual output in Linux and macOS. The default implementation is to exit the app, but you could implement this method in a subclass to change the behavior. See https://docs.python.org/3/library/signal.html#note-on-sigpipe for details.

### add_column

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Adds a column to the table with configurable header, footer, styles, alignment, overflow, and sizing options.

```APIDOC
## add_column(header='', footer='', header_style=None, highlight=None, footer_style=None, style=None, justify='left', vertical='top', overflow='ellipsis', width=None, min_width=None, max_width=None, ratio=None, no_wrap=False)

### Description
Add a column to the table.

### Parameters
- **header** (RenderableType, optional) - Text or renderable for the header. Defaults to "".
- **footer** (RenderableType, optional) - Text or renderable for the footer. Defaults to "".
- **header_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Style for the header, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Whether to highlight the text. The default of None uses the value of the table (self) object.
- **footer_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Style for the footer, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Style for the column cells, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **justify** (JustifyMethod, optional) - Alignment for cells. Defaults to "left".
- **vertical** (VerticalAlignMethod, optional) - Vertical alignment, one of "top", "middle", or "bottom". Defaults to "top".
- **overflow** (OverflowMethod) - Overflow method: "crop", "fold", "ellipsis". Defaults to "ellipsis".
- **width** (int, optional) - Desired width of column in characters, or None to fit to contents. Defaults to None.
- **min_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Minimum width of column, or None for no minimum. Defaults to None.
- **max_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Maximum width of column, or None for no maximum. Defaults to None.
- **ratio** (int, optional) - Flexible ratio for the column (requires Table.expand or Table.width). Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (bool, optional) - Set to True to disable wrapping of this column.

### Returns
None
```

### render_message(record, message)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/logging.rst

Renders the message text of a log record into a Rich Text object, which can then be displayed by the console.

```APIDOC
## render_message(record, message)

### Description
Renders the message text of a log record into a Rich Text object, which can then be displayed by the console.

### Parameters
- **record** (LogRecord) - Required - The logging record.
- **message** (str) - Required - String containing the log message.

### Returns
- **ConsoleRenderable** - A renderable object to display the log message.

### Return Type
[ConsoleRenderable](console.md#rich.console.ConsoleRenderable)
```

### rich.protocol.rich_cast(renderable)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/protocol.rst

Casts an object to a renderable by calling its __rich__ method if present. This function recursively calls __rich__ to obtain the final renderable object.

```APIDOC
## rich.protocol.rich_cast(renderable)

### Description
Cast an object to a renderable by calling __rich__ if present.

### Parameters
- **renderable** (object) - A potentially renderable object.

### Returns
- **object** - The result of recursively calling __rich__.
```

log.error("123 will not be highlighted", extra={"highlighter": None})

--------------------------------

### Transient progress display

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Set transient=True on the Progress constructor to remove the progress display from the terminal on exit. Useful for minimal output when tasks complete.

```python
with Progress(transient=True) as progress:
    task = progress.add_task("Working", total=100)
    do_work(task)
```

--------------------------------

### rich.pretty.Node

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

A node in a repr tree, representing an atomic value or a container. Provides methods to check length constraints, iterate tokens, and render the node to a pretty repr string.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.Node

### Description
A node in a repr tree. May be atomic or a container.

### Constructor Parameters
- **key_repr** (str) - Required - The key representation.
- **value_repr** (str) - Required - The value representation.
- **open_brace** (str) - Required - The opening brace string.
- **close_brace** (str) - Required - The closing brace string.
- **empty** (str) - Required - The empty representation.
- **last** (bool) - Required - Whether this is the last node.
- **is_tuple** (bool) - Required - Whether this node represents a tuple.
- **is_namedtuple** (bool) - Required - Whether this node represents a namedtuple.
- **children** (List[Node] | None) - Required - The child nodes.
- **key_separator** (str) - Required - The separator between key and value.
- **separator** (str) - Required - The separator between items.

### Methods

#### check_length(start_length, max_length)

Check the length fits within a limit.

**Parameters:**
- **start_length** (int) - Required - Starting length of the line (indent, prefix, suffix).
- **max_length** (int) - Required - Maximum length.

**Returns:**
- **bool** - True if the node can be rendered within max length, otherwise False.

#### iter_tokens()

Generate tokens for this node.

**Returns:**
- **Iterable[str]** - An iterable of string tokens.

#### render(max_width=80, indent_size=4, expand_all=False)

Render the node to a pretty repr.

**Parameters:**
- **max_width** (int, optional) - Maximum width of the repr. Defaults to 80.
- **indent_size** (int, optional) - Size of indents. Defaults to 4.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Expand all levels. Defaults to False.

**Returns:**
- **str** - A repr string of the original object.
```

--------------------------------

### Splitter.divide(children, region)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/layout.rst

Abstract method to divide a region amongst several child layouts. Returns an iterable of (Layout, Region) tuples.

```APIDOC
## Splitter.divide(children, region)

### Description
Abstract method to divide a region amongst several child layouts. Returns an iterable of (Layout, Region) tuples.

### Signature
```python
def divide(children: Sequence[Layout], region: Region) -> Iterable[Tuple[Layout, Region]]
```

### Parameters
- **children** (Sequence[Layout]) - Required - A number of child layouts.
- **region** (Region) - Required - A rectangular region to divide.

### Returns
- **Iterable[Tuple[Layout, Region]]** - The division result.
```

### set_window_title(title)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Sets the title of the console terminal window. The title persists after the application exits, and some terminals may not support this feature.

```APIDOC
## set_window_title(title)

### Description
Set the title of the console terminal window. Note that there is no means within Rich of resetting the window title to its previous value, so the title will persist after the application exits. Some terminals may require configuration changes or may not support setting the title at all.

### Method
set_window_title

### Parameters
- **title** (str) - Required - The new title of the terminal window.

### Returns
- **bool** - True if the control code to change the terminal title was written, otherwise False. A return value of True does not guarantee that the window title has actually changed.
```

### Truncate containers with max_length

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

Truncate containers with more than the given number of elements. If data is truncated, Rich will display an ellipsis ... and the number of elements not shown.

```default
>>> pprint(locals(), max_length=2)
```

--------------------------------

### Suppress frames with suppress argument

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/traceback.rst

Exclude framework code (e.g., click) from Rich tracebacks by passing a list of modules or str paths to the suppress argument. Suppressed frames show only the line and file.

```default
import click
from rich.traceback import install
install(suppress=[click])
```

### rich.rule.Rule

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/rule.rst

Constructs a Rule renderable that draws a horizontal line with an optional title. The rule can be customized with characters for the line, style, end character, and title alignment.

```APIDOC
## rich.rule.Rule

### Description
Creates a Rule object, a console renderable that draws a horizontal rule (line). The rule can include a title and be customized in appearance.

### Constructor
`Rule(title='', characters='─', style='rule.line', end='\n', align='center')`

### Parameters
- **title** (Union[str, Text], optional) – Text to render in the rule. Defaults to “”.
- **characters** (str, optional) – Character(s) used to draw the line. Defaults to “─”.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) – Style of Rule. Defaults to “rule.line”.
- **end** (str, optional) – Character at end of Rule. Defaults to “\n”.
- **align** (str, optional) – How to align the title, one of “left”, “center”, or “right”. Defaults to “center”.
```

### add_row

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Adds a row of renderables to the table, with optional row style and section ending.

```APIDOC
## add_row(\*renderables, style=None, end_section=False)

### Description
Add a row of renderables to the table.

### Parameters
- **\*renderables** (None or renderable) - Each cell in a row must be a renderable object (including str), or None for a blank cell.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - An optional style to apply to the entire row. Defaults to None.
- **end_section** (bool, optional) - End a section and draw a line. Defaults to False.

### Raises
- **errors.NotRenderableError** - If you add something that can't be rendered.

### Returns
None
```

### Segment.set_shape(lines, width, height=None, style=None, new_lines=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/segment.rst

Classmethod that sets the shape of a list of lines (enclosing rectangle).

```APIDOC
## Segment.set_shape(lines, width, height=None, style=None, new_lines=False)

### Description
Set the shape of a list of lines (enclosing rectangle).

### Method
Classmethod

### Signature
`set_shape(lines, width, height=None, style=None, new_lines=False)`

### Parameters
- **lines** (List[List[Segment]]) - Required - A list of lines.
- **width** (int) - Required - Desired width.
- **height** (int | None) - Optional - Desired height or None for no change.

### Return type

<!-- topic: tables -->
### Create a table with rich.table.Table

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Creates a table with the Table class, showing header styling, column alignment, and console markup in cells. The Table resizes columns to fit the terminal width, wrapping text as needed.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.table import Table

console = Console()

table = Table(show_header=True, header_style="bold magenta")
table.add_column("Date", style="dim", width=12)
table.add_column("Title")
table.add_column("Production Budget", justify="right")
table.add_column("Box Office", justify="right")
table.add_row(
    "Dec 20, 2019", "Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker", "$275,000,000", "$375,126,118"
)
table.add_row(
    "May 25, 2018",
    "[red]Solo[/red]: A Star Wars Story",
    "$275,000,000",
    "$393,151,347",
)
table.add_row(
    "Dec 15, 2017",
    "Star Wars Ep. VIII: The Last Jedi",
    "$262,000,000",
    "[bold]$1,332,539,889[/bold]",
)

console.print(table)
```

--------------------------------

### Create table with positional column arguments

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/tables.rst

Constructs a table by passing column titles as positional arguments to the Table constructor. This allows setting only the column text.

```default
table = Table("Released", "Title", "Box Office", title="Star Wars Movies")

```

### Table(*headers, title=None, caption=None, width=None, min_width=None, box=Box(...), safe_box=None, padding=(0, 1), collapse_padding=False, pad_edge=True, expand=False, show_header=True, show_footer=False, show_edge=True, show_lines=False, leading=0, style='none', row_styles=None, header_style='table.header', footer_style='table.footer', border_style=None, title_style=None, caption_style=None, title_justify='center', caption_justify='center', highlight=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Constructor for the Table class, which creates a console renderable to draw a table with configurable headers, styling, and layout.

```APIDOC
## Table(*headers, title=None, caption=None, width=None, min_width=None, box=Box(...), safe_box=None, padding=(0, 1), collapse_padding=False, pad_edge=True, expand=False, show_header=True, show_footer=False, show_edge=True, show_lines=False, leading=0, style='none', row_styles=None, header_style='table.header', footer_style='table.footer', border_style=None, title_style=None, caption_style=None, title_justify='center', caption_justify='center', highlight=False)

### Description
Creates a console renderable that draws a table. This constructor initializes a table with optional headers, title, caption, and various styling and layout options.

### Method
Constructor

### Parameters
- **\*headers** (Union[Column, str]) - Optional - Column headers, either as a string or a Column instance.
- **title** (Union[str, Text], optional) - Optional - The title of the table rendered at the top. Defaults to None.
- **caption** (Union[str, Text], optional) - Optional - The table caption rendered below. Defaults to None.
- **width** (int, optional) - Optional - The width in characters of the table, or None to automatically fit. Defaults to None.
- **min_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - The minimum width of the table, or None for no minimum. Defaults to None.
- **box** (box.Box, optional) - Optional - One of the constants in box.py used to draw the edges, or None for no box lines. Defaults to box.HEAVY_HEAD.
- **safe_box** (Optional[bool], optional) - Optional - Disable box characters that don't display on windows legacy terminal with raster fonts. Defaults to True.
- **padding** (PaddingDimensions, optional) - Optional - Padding for cells (top, right, bottom, left). Defaults to (0, 1).
- **collapse_padding** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable collapsing of padding around cells. Defaults to False.
- **pad_edge** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable padding of edge cells. Defaults to True.
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand the table to fit the available space if True, otherwise the table width will be auto-calculated. Defaults to False.
- **show_header** (bool, optional) - Optional - Show a header row. Defaults to True.
- **show_footer** (bool, optional) - Optional - Show a footer row. Defaults to False.
- **show_edge** (bool, optional) - Optional - Draw a box around the outside of the table. Defaults to True.
- **show_lines** (bool, optional) - Optional - Draw lines between every row. Defaults to False.
- **leading** (int, optional) - Optional - Number of blank lines between rows (precludes show_lines). Defaults to 0.
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Default style for the table. Defaults to "none".
- **row_styles** (List[Union, str], optional) - Optional - Optional list of row styles, if more than one style is given then the styles will alternate. Defaults to None.
- **header_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the header. Defaults to "table.header".
- **footer_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the footer. Defaults to "table.footer".
- **border_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the border. Defaults to None.
- **title_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the title. Defaults to None.
- **caption_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - Style of the caption. Defaults to None.
- **title_justify** (str, optional) - Optional - Justify method for title. Defaults to "center".
- **caption_justify** (str, optional) - Optional - Justify method for caption. Defaults to "center".
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Optional - Highlight cell contents (if str). Defaults to False.

### Returns
A Table instance that can be rendered as a console renderable.
```

--------------------------------

### Handle empty table with conditional print

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/tables.rst

Checks if the table has columns before printing to avoid a blank line when the data source has no columns. Prints a styled message instead.

```default
if table.columns:
    print(table)
else:
    print("[i]No data...[/i]")

```

### Create and print a Star Wars movies table

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/tables.rst

Constructs a Table with columns 'Released', 'Title', and 'Box Office', styled with colors and alignment. Adds four rows of Star Wars movie data and prints the table to the console. Rich calculates column widths and wraps text to fit the terminal.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.table import Table

table = Table(title="Star Wars Movies")

table.add_column("Released", justify="right", style="cyan", no_wrap=True)
table.add_column("Title", style="magenta")
table.add_column("Box Office", justify="right", style="green")

table.add_row("Dec 20, 2019", "Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker", "$952,110,690")
table.add_row("May 25, 2018", "Solo: A Star Wars Story", "$393,151,347")
table.add_row("Dec 15, 2017", "Star Wars Ep. V111: The Last Jedi", "$1,332,539,889")
table.add_row("Dec 16, 2016", "Rogue One: A Star Wars Story", "$1,332,439,889")

console = Console()
console.print(table)
```

```console
                           Star Wars Movies                           
┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓
┃     Released ┃ Title                             ┃     Box Office ┃
┡━━━━━━━━━━━━━━╇━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━╇━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┩
│ Dec 20, 2019 │ Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker  │   $952,110,690 │
│ May 25, 2018 │ Solo: A Star Wars Story           │   $393,151,347 │
│ Dec 15, 2017 │ Star Wars Ep. V111: The Last Jedi │ $1,332,539,889 │
│ Dec 16, 2016 │ Rogue One: A Star Wars Story      │ $1,332,439,889 │
└──────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┴────────────────┘
```

### grid

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Creates a grid-style table with no lines, headers, or footer, with configurable padding and expansion.

```APIDOC
## grid(\*headers, padding=0, collapse_padding=True, pad_edge=False, expand=False) (classmethod)

### Description
Get a table with no lines, headers, or footer.

### Parameters
- **\*headers** (Union[Column, str]) - Column headers, either as a string, or Column instance.
- **padding** (PaddingDimensions, optional) - Get padding around cells. Defaults to 0.
- **collapse_padding** (bool, optional) - Enable collapsing of padding around cells. Defaults to True.
- **pad_edge** (bool, optional) - Enable padding around edges of table. Defaults to False.
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Expand the table to fit the available space if True, otherwise the table width will be auto-calculated. Defaults to False.

### Returns
Table - A table instance.
```

## rich.table.Column

### Description
Configuration options for a table column, including width, wrapping, overflow, alignment, and style.

Minimum width of column, or `None` for no minimum.

The style of the column.

How to vertically align content ("top", "middle", or "bottom").

Width of the column, or `None` to auto calculate width.

### add_row

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Adds a row of renderables to the table, with optional row style and section ending.

```APIDOC
## add_row(\*renderables, style=None, end_section=False)

### Description
Add a row of renderables to the table.

### Parameters
- **\*renderables** (None or renderable) - Each cell in a row must be a renderable object (including str), or None for a blank cell.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - An optional style to apply to the entire row. Defaults to None.
- **end_section** (bool, optional) - End a section and draw a line. Defaults to False.

### Raises
- **errors.NotRenderableError** - If you add something that can't be rendered.

### Returns
None
```

### Progress with custom table column ratios

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Demonstrates a progress bar where the description column takes one third of the terminal width and the bar takes the remaining two thirds, using table_column with ratio values. Includes a loop that tracks 100 items and prints each step.

```python
from time import sleep

from rich.table import Column
from rich.progress import Progress, BarColumn, TextColumn

text_column = TextColumn("{task.description}", table_column=Column(ratio=1))
bar_column = BarColumn(bar_width=None, table_column=Column(ratio=2))
progress = Progress(text_column, bar_column, expand=True)

with progress:
    for n in progress.track(range(100)):
        progress.print(n)
        sleep(0.1)
```

--------------------------------

### Customize progress display by overriding get_renderables

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Override get_renderables in a Progress subclass to wrap the progress table in a Panel. This example yields a Panel containing the tasks table.

```python
from rich.panel import Panel
from rich.progress import Progress

class MyProgress(Progress):
    def get_renderables(self):
        yield Panel(self.make_tasks_table(self.tasks))
```

### Column

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Defines a column within a Table. The constructor accepts various parameters to control the column's header, footer, style, alignment, width, and other display properties.

```APIDOC
## Column

### Description
Defines a column within a Table. The constructor accepts various parameters to control the column's header, footer, style, alignment, width, and other display properties.

### Constructor Parameters
- **header** (RenderableType) - Required - Renderable for the header (typically a string).
- **footer** (RenderableType) - Required - Renderable for the footer (typically a string).
- **header_style** (StyleType) - Optional - The style of the header. Defaults to ''.
- **footer_style** (StyleType) - Optional - The style of the footer. Defaults to ''.
- **style** (StyleType) - Optional - Default style for the column. Defaults to ''.
- **justify** (JustifyMethod) - Optional - How to justify text within the column ("left", "center", "right", or "full"). Defaults to 'left'.
- **vertical** (VerticalAlignMethod) - Optional - Vertical alignment of the column content. Defaults to 'top'.
- **overflow** (OverflowMethod) - Optional - Overflow handling for the column. Defaults to 'ellipsis'.
- **width** (int) - Optional - The width in characters of the column, or None to automatically fit. Defaults to None.
- **min_width** (int) - Optional - The minimum width of the column, or None for no minimum. Defaults to None.
- **max_width** (int) - Optional - Maximum width of column, or None for no maximum. Defaults to None.
- **ratio** (int) - Optional - The ratio of the column width relative to other columns. Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (bool) - Optional - Disable wrapping of text within the column. Defaults to False.
- **highlight** (bool) - Optional - Apply highlighter to column. Defaults to False.
- **_index** (int) - Optional - Internal index of the column. Defaults to 0.
- **_cells** (List[RenderableType]) - Optional - Internal list of cells in the column. Defaults to an empty list.

### Properties
- **cells** (Iterable[RenderableType]) - Get all cells in the column, not including header.
- **flexible** (bool) - Check if this column is flexible.
- **footer** (RenderableType) - Renderable for the footer (typically a string).
- **footer_style** (StyleType) - The style of the footer.
- **header** (RenderableType) - Renderable for the header (typically a string).
- **header_style** (StyleType) - The style of the header.
- **highlight** (bool) - Apply highlighter to column. Defaults to False.
- **justify** (JustifyMethod) - How to justify text within the column ("left", "center", "right", or "full").
- **max_width** (int) - Maximum width of column, or None for no maximum.

### Methods
#### copy()
Return a copy of this Column.

**Return type:** Column

```

--------------------------------

### Implement __rich_console__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

For more advanced rendering, add a __rich_console__ method that accepts a Console and ConsoleOptions and returns an iterable of renderable objects. Using yield makes it a generator. This example renders a Student as a table.

```default
from dataclasses import dataclass
from rich.console import Console, ConsoleOptions, RenderResult
from rich.table import Table

@dataclass
class Student:
    id: int
    name: str
    age: int
    def __rich_console__(self, console: Console, options: ConsoleOptions) -> RenderResult:
        yield f"[b]Student:[/b] #{self.id}"
        my_table = Table("Attribute", "Value")
        my_table.add_row("name", self.name)
        my_table.add_row("age", str(self.age))
        yield my_table
```

--------------------------------

### add_column

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/table.rst

Adds a column to the table with configurable header, footer, styles, alignment, overflow, and sizing options.

```APIDOC
## add_column(header='', footer='', header_style=None, highlight=None, footer_style=None, style=None, justify='left', vertical='top', overflow='ellipsis', width=None, min_width=None, max_width=None, ratio=None, no_wrap=False)

### Description
Add a column to the table.

### Parameters
- **header** (RenderableType, optional) - Text or renderable for the header. Defaults to "".
- **footer** (RenderableType, optional) - Text or renderable for the footer. Defaults to "".
- **header_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Style for the header, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Whether to highlight the text. The default of None uses the value of the table (self) object.
- **footer_style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Style for the footer, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Style for the column cells, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **justify** (JustifyMethod, optional) - Alignment for cells. Defaults to "left".
- **vertical** (VerticalAlignMethod, optional) - Vertical alignment, one of "top", "middle", or "bottom". Defaults to "top".
- **overflow** (OverflowMethod) - Overflow method: "crop", "fold", "ellipsis". Defaults to "ellipsis".
- **width** (int, optional) - Desired width of column in characters, or None to fit to contents. Defaults to None.
- **min_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Minimum width of column, or None for no minimum. Defaults to None.
- **max_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Maximum width of column, or None for no maximum. Defaults to None.
- **ratio** (int, optional) - Flexible ratio for the column (requires Table.expand or Table.width). Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (bool, optional) - Set to True to disable wrapping of this column.

### Returns
None
```

```

--------------------------------

### Update the renderable with Live.update()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Change the renderable on-the-fly by calling the update() method. Useful when the information is too dynamic to generate by updating a single renderable. The example generates a new table each iteration and updates the live display.

```default
import random
import time

from rich.live import Live
from rich.table import Table


def generate_table() -> Table:
    """Make a new table."""
    table = Table()
    table.add_column("ID")
    table.add_column("Value")
    table.add_column("Status")

    for row in range(random.randint(2, 6)):
        value = random.random() * 100
        table.add_row(
            f"{row}", f"{value:3.2f}", "[red]ERROR" if value < 50 else "[green]SUCCESS"
        )
    return table


with Live(generate_table(), refresh_per_second=4) as live:
    for _ in range(40):
        time.sleep(0.4)
        live.update(generate_table())
```

### Implement __rich__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

Implement a __rich__ method on your class to customize console output. It accepts no arguments and should return an object Rich knows how to render, such as a Text or Table. Returning a plain string renders as Console Markup.

```default
class MyObject:
    def __rich__(self) -> str:
        return "[bold cyan]MyObject()"
```

### Log with Console.log and log_locals

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Demonstrates the Console.log() method, which renders a column for the current time and the file/line of the call. By default, Rich does syntax highlighting for Python structures and repr strings, and pretty-prints collections. The log_locals argument outputs a table of local variables where log was called.

```python
from rich.console import Console
console = Console()

test_data = [
    {"jsonrpc": "2.0", "method": "sum", "params": [None, 1, 2, 4, False, True], "id": "1",},
    {"jsonrpc": "2.0", "method": "notify_hello", "params": [7]},
    {"jsonrpc": "2.0", "method": "subtract", "params": [42, 23], "id": "2"},
]

def test_log():
    enabled = False
    context = {
        "foo": "bar",
    }
    movies = ["Deadpool", "Rise of the Skywalker"]
    console.log("Hello from", console, "!")
    console.log(test_data, log_locals=True)


test_log()
```

### Rich API Reference Index

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference.rst

An index of the Rich library's public API, listing classes and their methods for styled text, tables, syntax highlighting, and more.

```APIDOC
## Rich API Reference

### Description
This is a reference index for the Rich library, providing links to detailed documentation for each class and method.

### Classes and Methods

#### rich.styled
- **Styled** - A class for applying styles to content.

#### rich.syntax
- **Syntax** - A class for syntax highlighting.
  - **Syntax.default_lexer** - The default lexer used for syntax highlighting.
  - **Syntax.from_path()** - Create a Syntax object from a file path.
  - **Syntax.get_theme()** - Get the syntax highlighting theme.
  - **Syntax.guess_lexer()** - Guess the lexer for a given code string.
  - **Syntax.highlight()** - Highlight the code with syntax highlighting.
  - **Syntax.lexer** - The lexer used for syntax highlighting.
  - **Syntax.stylize_range()** - Apply styles to a range of text.

#### rich.table
- **Column** - A class representing a table column.
  - **Column.cells** - The cells in the column.
  - **Column.copy()** - Copy the column.
  - **Column.flexible** - Whether the column is flexible.
  - **Column.footer** - The footer text.
  - **Column.footer_style** - The style for the footer.
  - **Column.header** - The header text.
  - **Column.header_style** - The style for the header.
  - **Column.highlight** - Whether to highlight the column.
  - **Column.justify** - The justification of the column.
  - **Column.max_width** - The maximum width of the column.
  - **Column.min_width** - The minimum width of the column.
  - **Column.no_wrap** - Whether to disable wrapping.
  - **Column.overflow** - The overflow behavior.
  - **Column.ratio** - The ratio of the column.
  - **Column.style** - The style of the column.
  - **Column.vertical** - The vertical alignment.
  - **Column.width** - The width of the column.
- **Row** - A class representing a table row.
  - **Row.end_section** - Whether the row ends a section.
  - **Row.style** - The style of the row.
- **Table** - A class for creating tables.
  - **Table.add_column()** - Add a column to the table.
  - **Table.add_row()** - Add a row to the table.
  - **Table.add_section()** - Add a section to the table.
  - **Table.expand** - Whether the table expands to fill available space.
  - **Table.get_row_style()** - Get the style of a row.
  - **Table.grid()** - Create a grid table.
  - **Table.padding** - The padding of the table.
  - **Table.row_count** - The number of rows in the table.

#### rich.text
- **Text** - A class for styled text.
  - **Text.align()** - Align the text.
  - **Text.append()** - Append text.
  - **Text.append_text()** - Append another Text object.
  - **Text.append_tokens()** - Append tokens.
  - **Text.apply_meta()** - Apply metadata to the text.
  - **Text.assemble()** - Assemble text from parts.
  - **Text.blank_copy()** - Create a blank copy of the text.
  - **Text.cell_len** - The cell length of the text.
  - **Text.copy()** - Copy the text.
  - **Text.copy_styles()** - Copy styles from another text.
  - **Text.detect_indentation()** - Detect the indentation of the text.
  - **Text.divide()** - Divide the text into parts.
  - **Text.expand_tabs()** - Expand tabs in the text.
  - **Text.extend_style()** - Extend the style of the text.
  - **Text.fit()** - Fit the text to a width.
  - **Text.from_ansi()** - Create a Text object from ANSI codes.
  - **Text.from_markup()** - Create a Text object from markup.
  - **Text.get_style_at_offset()** - Get the style at a given offset.
  - **Text.highlight_regex()** - Highlight regex matches.
  - **Text.highlight_words()** - Highlight words.
  - **Text.join()** - Join text with a separator.
  - **Text.markup** - The markup of the text.
  - **Text.on()** - Apply a style to the text.
  - **Text.pad()** - Pad the text.
  - **Text.pad_left()** - Pad the left side of the text.
  - **Text.pad_right()** - Pad the right side of the text.
  - **Text.plain** - The plain text content.
```

--------------------------------

### Print to live.console in a Live context

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Use the internal console of a Live instance to print messages above the live display. The example updates a table 4 times per second and prints progress messages via live.console.print.

```python
import time

from rich.live import Live
from rich.table import Table

table = Table()
table.add_column("Row ID")
table.add_column("Description")
table.add_column("Level")

with Live(table, refresh_per_second=4) as live:  # update 4 times a second to feel fluid
    for row in range(12):
        live.console.print(f"Working on row #{row}")
        time.sleep(0.4)
        table.add_row(f"{row}", f"description {row}", "[red]ERROR")
```

python -m rich.live

### Creating a grid with Table.grid for layout

### Columns.__init__

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/columns.rst

Initializes a Columns object with the given renderables and layout options. This constructor allows customization of width, padding, expansion, alignment, and other display properties.

```APIDOC
## Columns.__init__

### Description
Initializes a Columns object to display renderables in neat columns. Accepts an iterable of renderables and various layout options.

### Method
Constructor

### Signature
`Columns(renderables=None, padding=(0, 1), width=None, expand=False, equal=False, column_first=False, right_to_left=False, align=None, title=None)`

### Parameters
- **renderables** (Iterable[RenderableType]) - Optional - Any number of Rich renderables (including str).
- **width** (int, optional) - Optional - The desired width of the columns, or None to auto detect. Defaults to None.
- **padding** (PaddingDimensions, optional) - Optional - Optional padding around cells. Defaults to (0, 1).
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand columns to full width. Defaults to False.
- **equal** (bool, optional) - Optional - Arrange in to equal sized columns. Defaults to False.
- **column_first** (bool, optional) - Optional - Align items from top to bottom (rather than left to right). Defaults to False.
- **right_to_left** (bool, optional) - Optional - Start column from right hand side. Defaults to False.
- **align** (str, optional) - Optional - Align value ("left", "right", or "center") or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **title** (TextType, optional) - Optional - Optional title for Columns.

### Returns
None
```

--------------------------------

### TableRowElement.on_child_close(context, child)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/markdown.rst

Called when a child element is closed, allowing a parent element to take over rendering of its children. Returns True to render the element, or False to not render the element.

```APIDOC
## TableRowElement.on_child_close(context, child)

### Description
Called when a child element is closed. This method allows a parent element to take over rendering of its children.

### Method
on_child_close

### Parameters
- **context** (MarkdownContext) – The markdown context.
- **child** (MarkdownElement) – The child markdown element.

### Returns
bool – Return True to render the element, or False to not render the element.

```

### Implement __rich_measure__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

Supply a __rich_measure__ method to let Rich know how many characters an object will take up when rendering. It accepts a Console and ConsoleOptions and returns a Measurement with minimum and maximum characters. This example returns a minimum of 8 for a chess board and the maximum available width.

```default
class ChessBoard:
    def __rich_measure__(self, console: Console, options: ConsoleOptions) -> Measurement:
        return Measurement(8, options.max_width)
```

### Create a basic Panel with Rich

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/panel.rst

Construct a Panel with a renderable as the first positional argument to draw a border around it. The example prints a panel containing the text 'Hello, [red]World!'.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.panel import Panel
print(Panel("Hello, [red]World!"))
```

### Panel(renderable, box=Box(...), title=None, title_align='center', subtitle=None, subtitle_align='center', safe_box=None, expand=True, style='none', border_style='none', width=None, height=None, padding=(0, 1), highlight=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/panel.rst

Creates a console renderable that draws a border around its contents. Used when you want to display content in a styled box with optional title and subtitle.

```APIDOC
## Panel

### Description
A console renderable that draws a border around its contents.

### Method
Constructor (class instantiation)

### Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - Required - A console renderable object.
- **box** (Box) - Optional - A Box instance that defines the look of the border. Defaults to box.ROUNDED.
- **title** (Optional[TextType], optional) - Optional - Optional title displayed in panel header. Defaults to None.
- **title_align** (AlignMethod, optional) - Optional - Alignment of title. Defaults to "center".
- **subtitle** (Optional[TextType], optional) - Optional - Optional subtitle displayed in panel footer. Defaults to None.
- **subtitle_align** (AlignMethod, optional) - Optional - Alignment of subtitle. Defaults to "center".
- **safe_box** (bool, optional) - Optional - Disable box characters that don't display on windows legacy terminal with raster fonts. Defaults to True.
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Optional - If True the panel will stretch to fill the console width, otherwise it will be sized to fit the contents. Defaults to True.
- **style** (str, optional) - Optional - The style of the panel (border and contents). Defaults to "none".
- **border_style** (str, optional) - Optional - The style of the border. Defaults to "none".
- **width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Optional width of panel. Defaults to None to auto-detect.
- **height** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Optional height of panel. Defaults to None to auto-detect.
- **padding** (Optional[PaddingDimensions]) - Optional - Optional padding around renderable. Defaults to 0.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable automatic highlighting of panel title (if str). Defaults to False.

### Example
```pycon
>>> console.print(Panel("Hello, World!"))
```
```

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

from rich import print

### Basic Columns example: clone of ls command

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/columns.rst

Constructs a Columns instance from a directory listing and prints it, using equal=True and expand=True to space the columns evenly. Requires a directory argument on the command line; otherwise prints usage.

```python
import os
import sys

from rich import print
from rich.columns import Columns

if len(sys.argv) < 2:
    print("Usage: python columns.py DIRECTORY")
else:
    directory = os.listdir(sys.argv[1])
    columns = Columns(directory, equal=True, expand=True)
    print(columns)
```

### Assemble Text with styled parts using assemble

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Combines strings and style pairs into a Text instance using the assemble method. This example is equivalent to the previous ANSI example, producing a Text with 'Hello' in bold magenta and ', World!' plain.

```default
text = Text.assemble(("Hello", "bold magenta"), ", World!")
console.print(text)
```

```python
import os
import sys

from rich import print
from rich.columns import Columns

directory = os.listdir(sys.argv[1])
print(Columns(directory))

### Split layout into columns

--------------------------------

### Update alternate screen with renderable using console.screen()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Update the alternate screen with a renderable using screen.update(). This allows Rich to crop the contents to fit the screen without scrolling. The example displays a countdown in a panel with a blinking message.

```python
from time import sleep

from rich.console import Console
from rich.align import Align
from rich.text import Text
from rich.panel import Panel

console = Console()

with console.screen(style="bold white on red") as screen:
    for count in range(5, 0, -1):
        text = Align.center(
            Text.from_markup(f"[blink]Don't Panic![/blink]\n{count}", justify="center"),
            vertical="middle",
        )
        screen.update(Panel(text))
        sleep(1)
```

--------------------------------

### Update layout renderable with Layout.update

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/layout.rst

Sets or replaces the current renderable of the 'left' layout using the update method. After updating, the layout is printed to show the result.

```python
layout["left"].update(
    "The mystery of life isn't a problem to solve, but a reality to experience."
)
print(layout)
```

### Group multiple renderables with Group class

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/group.rst

Use the Group class to combine two Panel renderables into a single renderable, which you can then wrap in another Panel for display. Child renderables are passed as positional arguments.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.console import Group
from rich.panel import Panel

panel_group = Group(
    Panel("Hello", style="on blue"),
    Panel("World", style="on red"),
)
print(Panel(panel_group))
```

### Demonstrate overflow methods with Console.print

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Demonstrates the fold, crop, and ellipsis overflow methods using a Console with width=14. The default is 'fold', which wraps excess characters to new lines; 'crop' truncates at the line end; 'ellipsis' truncates and appends an ellipsis character. The output shows each method's effect on the long word 'supercalifragilisticexpialidocious'.

```python
from typing import List
from rich.console import Console, OverflowMethod

console = Console(width=14)
supercali = "supercalifragilisticexpialidocious"

overflow_methods: List[OverflowMethod] = ["fold", "crop", "ellipsis"]
for overflow in overflow_methods:
    console.rule(overflow)
    console.print(supercali, overflow=overflow, style="bold blue")
    console.print()
```

--------------------------------

### TaskProgressColumn

## TaskProgressColumn

### Description

- **highlighter** (Optional[Highlighter], optional) - Highlighter to apply to output. Defaults to None.

### Yield Segment objects for low-level rendering

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/protocol.rst

For complete control over rendering, yield Segment objects consisting of text and an optional Style. This example writes multi-colored text for a MyObject instance.

```default
class MyObject:
    def __rich_console__(self, console: Console, options: ConsoleOptions) -> RenderResult:
        yield Segment("My", Style(color="magenta"))
        yield Segment("Object", Style(color="green"))
        yield Segment("()", Style(color="cyan"))
```

Called when a child element is closed. This method allows a parent element to take over rendering of its children.

### fit(renderable, box=Box(...), title=None, title_align='center', subtitle=None, subtitle_align='center', safe_box=None, style='none', border_style='none', width=None, height=None, padding=(0, 1), highlight=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/panel.rst

An alternative constructor that sets expand=False, causing the panel to fit its contents instead of stretching to fill the console width. This is a classmethod callable on the Panel class.

```APIDOC
## Panel.fit

### Description
An alternative constructor that sets expand=False.

### Method
Classmethod (alternative constructor)

### Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - Required - A console renderable object.
- **box** (Box) - Optional - A Box instance that defines the look of the border. Defaults to box.ROUNDED.
- **title** (str | Text | None) - Optional - Optional title displayed in panel header.
- **title_align** (Literal['left', 'center', 'right']) - Optional - Alignment of title.
- **subtitle** (str | Text | None) - Optional - Optional subtitle displayed in panel footer.
- **subtitle_align** (Literal['left', 'center', 'right']) - Optional - Alignment of subtitle.
- **safe_box** (bool | None) - Optional - Disable box characters that don't display on windows legacy terminal with raster fonts.
- **style** (str | Style) - Optional - The style of the panel (border and contents).
- **border_style** (str | Style) - Optional - The style of the border.
- **width** (int | None) - Optional - Optional width of panel.
- **height** (int | None) - Optional - Optional height of panel.
- **padding** (int | Tuple[int] | Tuple[int, int] | Tuple[int, int, int, int]) - Optional - Optional padding around renderable.
- **highlight** (bool) - Optional - Enable automatic highlighting of panel title (if str).

### Return Type
Panel
```

### render

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Render an object in to an iterable of Segment instances.

```APIDOC
## render(renderable, options=None)

### Description
Render an object in to an iterable of Segment instances. This method contains the logic for rendering objects with the console protocol. You are unlikely to need to use it directly, unless you are extending the library.

### Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - An object supporting the console protocol, or an object that may be converted to a string.
- **options** (ConsoleOptions, optional) - An options object, or None to use self.options. Defaults to None.

### Returns
Iterable[Segment] - An iterable of segments that may be rendered.
```

### rich.pretty.pprint

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

Function to pretty print an object to the console with configurable options for abbreviation, truncation, depth, and indentation guides.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.pprint(_object, *, console=None, indent_guides=True, max_length=None, max_string=None, max_depth=None, expand_all=False)

### Description
A convenience function for pretty printing an object to the console.

### Parameters
- **_object** (Any) - Required - Object to pretty print.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional - Console instance, or None to use default. Defaults to None.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of strings before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depth for nested data structures, or None for unlimited depth. Defaults to None.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable indentation guides. Defaults to True.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand all containers. Defaults to False.

### Return Type
None
```

### rich.pretty.Pretty

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

A rich renderable that pretty prints an object. It accepts various formatting options to control indentation, wrapping, truncation, and expansion.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.Pretty

### Description
A rich renderable that pretty prints an object.

### Constructor Parameters
- **_object** (Any) - Required - An object to pretty print.
- **highlighter** (HighlighterType, optional) - Highlighter object to apply to result, or None for ReprHighlighter. Defaults to None.
- **indent_size** (int, optional) - Number of spaces in indent. Defaults to 4.
- **justify** (JustifyMethod, optional) - Justify method, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **overflow** (OverflowMethod, optional) - Overflow method, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (Optional[bool], optional) - Disable word wrapping. Defaults to False.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Enable indentation guides. Defaults to False.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Maximum depth of nested data structures, or None for no maximum. Defaults to None.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Expand all containers. Defaults to False.
- **margin** (int, optional) - Subtract a margin from width to force containers to expand earlier. Defaults to 0.
- **insert_line** (bool, optional) - Insert a new line if the output has multiple new lines. Defaults to False.
```

--------------------------------

### Align(renderable, align='left', style=None, vertical=None, pad=True, width=None, height=None)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/align.rst

Align a renderable by adding spaces if necessary. Supports horizontal alignment (left, center, right) and optional vertical alignment.

```APIDOC
## Align(renderable, align='left', style=None, vertical=None, pad=True, width=None, height=None)

### Description
Align a renderable by adding spaces if necessary. Supports horizontal alignment (left, center, right) and optional vertical alignment.

### Constructor Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - Required - A console renderable.
- **align** (AlignMethod) - Optional - One of "left", "center", or "right". Defaults to "left".
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Optional - An optional style to apply to the background.
- **vertical** (Optional[VerticalAlignMethod], optional) - Optional - Optional vertical align, one of "top", "middle", or "bottom". Defaults to None.
- **pad** (bool, optional) - Optional - Pad the right with spaces. Defaults to True.
- **width** (int, optional) - Optional - Restrict contents to given width, or None to use default width. Defaults to None.
- **height** (int, optional) - Optional - Set height of align renderable, or None to fit to contents. Defaults to None.

### Raises
- **ValueError** - if `align` is not one of the expected values.

### Example
```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.align import Align
from rich.panel import Panel

console = Console()
# Create a panel 20 characters wide
p = Panel("Hello, [b]World[/b]!", style="on green", width=20)

# Renders the panel centered in the terminal
console.print(Align(p, align="center"))
```
```

### Split layout into panels with Layout.split

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/layout.rst

Divides the 'right' layout into two panels using the split method. The first positional argument to Layout can be any Rich renderable, which will be sized to fit within the layout's area.

```python
from rich.panel import Panel

layout["right"].split(
    Layout(Panel("Hello")),
    Layout(Panel("World!"))
)
```

### Padding(renderable, pad=(0, 0, 0, 0), style='none', expand=True)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/padding.rst

Creates a Padding instance that draws space around the given renderable. The pad parameter can be specified in CSS style with 1, 2, or 4 integers.

```APIDOC
## Padding(renderable, pad=(0, 0, 0, 0), style='none', expand=True)

### Description
Draw space around content. The pad parameter can be specified with 1, 2, or 4 integers (CSS style).

### Constructor Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType) - Required - String or other renderable.
- **pad** (Union[int, Tuple[int]]) - Optional - Padding for top, right, bottom, and left borders. May be specified with 1, 2, or 4 integers (CSS style). Defaults to (0, 0, 0, 0).
- **style** (Union[str, Style]) - Optional - Style for padding characters. Defaults to "none".
- **expand** (bool) - Optional - Expand padding to fit available width. Defaults to True.

### Example
```pycon
>>> print(Padding("Hello", (2, 4), style="on blue"))
```
```

## Segment.split_cells(cut)

### on(meta=None, **handlers)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/text.rst

Applies event handlers to the Text object, used by the Textual project. Returns self for chaining.

```APIDOC
## on(meta=None, **handlers)

### Description
Apply event handlers (used by Textual project).

### Method
on

### Parameters
#### Parameters
- **meta** (Dict[str, Any], optional) - Mapping of meta information.
- **\*\*handlers** - Keyword args are prefixed with "@" to defined handlers.

### Returns
Self is returned to method may be chained.

### Return Type
Text

### Example
```pycon
>>> from rich.text import Text
>>> text = Text("hello world")
>>> text.on(click="view.toggle('world')")
```
```

--------------------------------

### Console.print

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Prints objects to the console with rich formatting options.

```APIDOC
## Console.print

### Description
Print to the console. This method provides rich formatting options including justification, overflow handling, emoji, markup, and highlighting.

### Method
print

### Signature
`print(*objects, sep=' ', end='\n', style=None, justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=None, emoji=None, markup=None, highlight=None, width=None, height=None, crop=True, soft_wrap=None, new_line_start=False)`

### Parameters
- **objects** (*positional args*) - Required - Objects to log to the terminal.
- **sep** (*str*, optional) - String to write between print data. Defaults to " ".
- **end** (*str*, optional) - String to write at end of print data. Defaults to "\n".
- **style** (*Union[str, Style]*, optional) - A style to apply to output. Defaults to None.
- **justify** (*str*, optional) - Justify method: "default", "left", "right", "center", or "full". Defaults to `None`.
- **overflow** (*str*, optional) - Overflow method: "ignore", "crop", "fold", or "ellipsis". Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (*Optional[bool]*, optional) - Disable word wrapping. Defaults to None.
- **emoji** (*Optional[bool]*, optional) - Enable emoji code, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to `None`.
- **markup** (*Optional[bool]*, optional) - Enable markup, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to `None`.
- **highlight** (*Optional[bool]*, optional) - Enable automatic highlighting, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to `None`.
- **width** (*Optional[int]*, optional) - Width of output, or `None` to auto-detect. Defaults to `None`.
- **height** (*int | None*) - Height of output, or `None` to auto-detect.
- **crop** (*Optional[bool]*, optional) - Crop output to width of terminal. Defaults to True.
- **soft_wrap** (*bool*, optional) - Enable soft wrap mode which disables word wrapping and cropping of text or `None` for Console default. Defaults to `None`.
- **new_line_start** (*bool*, optional) - Insert a new line at the start if the output contains more than one line. Defaults to `False`.

### Return Type
None
```

--------------------------------

### Page output with console.pager()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Use the pager context manager to display long output. The pager command is determined by the MANPAGER or PAGER environment variables, with MANPAGER taking priority. Color is stripped by default; set styles=True to preserve ANSI styles if the pager supports them.

```python
from rich.__main__ import make_test_card
from rich.console import Console

console = Console()
with console.pager():
    console.print(make_test_card())
```

### JSON(json, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/json.rst

Constructor for the JSON renderable class that pretty prints JSON encoded data.

```APIDOC
## JSON(json, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)

### Description
Creates a renderable that pretty prints JSON encoded data.

### Constructor Parameters
- **json** (str) - Required - JSON encoded data.
- **indent** (Union[None, int, str]) - Optional - Number of characters to indent by. Defaults to 2.
- **highlight** (bool) - Optional - Enable highlighting. Defaults to True.
- **skip_keys** (bool) - Optional - Skip keys not of a basic type. Defaults to False.
- **ensure_ascii** (bool) - Optional - Escape all non-ascii characters. Defaults to False.
- **check_circular** (bool) - Optional - Check for circular references. Defaults to True.
- **allow_nan** (bool) - Optional - Allow NaN and Infinity values. Defaults to True.
- **default** (Callable) - Optional - A callable that converts values that cannot be encoded into something that can be JSON encoded. Defaults to None.
- **sort_keys** (bool) - Optional - Sort dictionary keys. Defaults to False.

### Example
```python
from rich.json import JSON
json_renderable = JSON('{"key": "value"}')
```
```

--------------------------------

### Create a basic live display with Live context manager

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/live.rst

Construct a Live object with a renderable and use it as a context manager. The live display persists for the duration of the context. Update the renderable internally to update the display. The refresh_per_second parameter controls the update rate.

```default
import time

from rich.live import Live
from rich.table import Table

table = Table()
table.add_column("Row ID")
table.add_column("Description")
table.add_column("Level")

with Live(table, refresh_per_second=4):  # update 4 times a second to feel fluid
    for row in range(12):
        time.sleep(0.4)  # arbitrary delay
        # update the renderable internally
        table.add_row(f"{row}", f"description {row}", "[red]ERROR")
```

### render_lines

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Render objects in to a list of lines.

```APIDOC
## render_lines(renderable, options=None, style=None, pad=True, new_lines=False)

### Description
Render objects in to a list of lines. The output of render_lines is useful when further formatting of rendered console text is required, such as the Panel class which draws a border around any renderable object.

### Parameters
- **renderable** (ConsoleRenderable | RichCast | str) - Any object renderable in the console.
- **options** (ConsoleOptions | None, optional) - Console options, or None to use self.options. Default to None.
- **style** (Style | None, optional) - Optional style to apply to renderables. Defaults to None.
- **pad** (bool, optional) - Pad lines shorter than render width. Defaults to True.
- **new_lines** (bool, optional) - Include newline characters at end of lines.

### Returns
List[List[Segment]] - A list of lines, where a line is a list of Segment objects.
```

### Fit Panel to content with Panel.fit

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/panel.rst

By default, panels extend to the full width of the terminal. Use expand=False or the fit() method to make the panel fit the content. The example uses Panel.fit to print a panel that fits its content.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.panel import Panel
print(Panel.fit("Hello, [red]World!"))
```

### Set Console style attribute

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Creates a Console with a style attribute that applies to all printed content. The style is set to "white on blue" and the print call demonstrates the effect.

```python
from rich.console import Console
blue_console = Console(style="white on blue")
blue_console.print("I'm blue. Da ba dee da ba di.")
```

### ListElement

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/markdown.rst

A list element. The create classmethod is a factory to create markdown elements, and on_child_close allows the parent element to take over rendering of its children.

```APIDOC
## ListElement

### Description
A list element.

### Class
`rich.markdown.ListElement`

### Constructor Parameters
- `list_type` (str)
- `list_start` (int | None)

### Methods

#### create(markdown, token) (classmethod)
Factory to create markdown element.

**Parameters:**
- `markdown` (Markdown) – The parent Markdown object.
- `token` (Token) – A node from markdown-it.

**Returns:**
A new markdown element

**Return type:**
MarkdownElement

#### on_child_close(context, child)
Called when a child element is closed. This method allows a parent element to take over rendering of its children.

**Parameters:**
- `context` (MarkdownContext) – The markdown context.
- `child` (MarkdownElement) – The child markdown element.

**Returns:**
Return True to render the element, or False to not render the element.

**Return type:**
bool
```
