### RichHandler(level=0, console=None, *, show_time=True, omit_repeated_times=True, show_level=True, show_path=True, enable_link_path=True, highlighter=None, markup=False, rich_tracebacks=False, tracebacks_width=None, tracebacks_code_width=88, tracebacks_extra_lines=3, tracebacks_theme=None, tracebacks_word_wrap=True, tracebacks_show_locals=False, tracebacks_suppress=(), tracebacks_max_frames=100, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, log_time_format='[%x %X]', keywords=None)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/logging.rst

Constructs a RichHandler instance. This handler renders log output with Rich, displaying time, level, message, and file in columns. The level is color coded and the message is syntax highlighted.

```APIDOC
## RichHandler(level=0, console=None, *, show_time=True, omit_repeated_times=True, show_level=True, show_path=True, enable_link_path=True, highlighter=None, markup=False, rich_tracebacks=False, tracebacks_width=None, tracebacks_code_width=88, tracebacks_extra_lines=3, tracebacks_theme=None, tracebacks_word_wrap=True, tracebacks_show_locals=False, tracebacks_suppress=(), tracebacks_max_frames=100, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, log_time_format='[%x %X]', keywords=None)

### Description
Constructs a RichHandler instance. This handler renders log output with Rich, displaying time, level, message, and file in columns. The level is color coded and the message is syntax highlighted.

### Parameters
- **level** (Union[int, str], optional) - Log level. Defaults to logging.NOTSET.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional console instance to write logs. Default will use a global console instance writing to stdout.
- **show_time** (bool, optional) - Show a column for the time. Defaults to True.
- **omit_repeated_times** (bool, optional) - Omit repetition of the same time. Defaults to True.
- **show_level** (bool, optional) - Show a column for the level. Defaults to True.
- **show_path** (bool, optional) - Show the path to the original log call. Defaults to True.
- **enable_link_path** (bool, optional) - Enable terminal link of path column to file. Defaults to True.
- **highlighter** (Highlighter, optional) - Highlighter to style log messages, or None to use ReprHighlighter. Defaults to None.
- **markup** (bool, optional) - Enable console markup in log messages. Defaults to False.
- **rich_tracebacks** (bool, optional) - Enable rich tracebacks with syntax highlighting and formatting. Defaults to False.
- **tracebacks_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Number of characters used to render tracebacks, or None for full width. Defaults to None.
- **tracebacks_code_width** (int, optional) - Number of code characters used to render tracebacks, or None for full width. Defaults to 88.
- **tracebacks_extra_lines** (int, optional) - Additional lines of code to render tracebacks, or None for full width. Defaults to None.
- **tracebacks_theme** (str, optional) - Override pygments theme used in traceback.
- **tracebacks_word_wrap** (bool, optional) - Enable word wrapping of long tracebacks lines. Defaults to True.
- **tracebacks_show_locals** (bool, optional) - Enable display of locals in tracebacks. Defaults to False.
- **tracebacks_suppress** (Sequence[Union[str, ModuleType]]) - Optional sequence of modules or paths to exclude from traceback.
- **tracebacks_max_frames** (int, optional) - Optional maximum number of frames returned by traceback.
- **locals_max_length** (int, optional) - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to 10.
- **locals_max_string** (int, optional) - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to 80.
- **log_time_format** (Union[str, TimeFormatterCallable], optional) - If `log_time` is enabled, either string for strftime or callable that formats the time. Defaults to "[%x %X] ".
- **keywords** (List[str], optional) - List of words to highlight instead of `RichHandler.KEYWORDS`.
```

--------------------------------

### from_ansi(text, style='', justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=None, end='\n', tab_size=8)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/text.rst

Class method to create a Text object from a string containing ANSI escape codes. Allows specifying base style, justification, overflow, wrapping, end character, and tab size.

```APIDOC
## from_ansi(text, style='', justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=None, end='\n', tab_size=8)

### Description
Create a Text object from a string containing ANSI escape codes.

### Method
from_ansi (classmethod)

### Parameters
#### Parameters
- **text** (str) - A string containing escape codes.
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Base style for text. Defaults to "".
- **justify** (str, optional) - Justify method: "left", "center", "full", "right". Defaults to None.
- **overflow** (str, optional) - Overflow method: "crop", "fold", "ellipsis". Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (bool, optional) - Disable text wrapping, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **end** (str, optional) - Character to end text with. Defaults to "\n".
- **tab_size** (int) - Number of spaces per tab, or `None` to use `console.tab_size`. Defaults to None.

### Return Type
Text
```

### Rich output with nested formatting

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

```default
{
    'gull': Bird(
        'gull',
        eats=[
            'fish',
            'chips',
            'ice cream',
            'sausage rolls'
        ]
    ),
    'penguin': Bird(
        'penguin',
        eats=['fish'],
        fly=False
    ),
    'dodo': Bird(
        'dodo',
        eats=['fruit'],
        fly=False,
        extinct=True
    )
}
```

--------------------------------

### Nested progress bars with track

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Creates an outer progress bar for a range of 10 and an inner transient progress bar for each letter in 'ABCDEF'. The inner bar appears below the outer bar and both update, with the nested bars updating according to the outer bar's refresh_per_second attribute.

```python
from rich.progress import track
from time import sleep


for count in track(range(10)):
    for letter in track("ABCDEF", transient=True):
        print(f"Stage {count}{letter}")
        sleep(0.1)
    sleep(0.1)

```

### Live(renderable=None, console=None, screen=False, auto_refresh=True, refresh_per_second=4, transient=False, redirect_stdout=True, redirect_stderr=True, vertical_overflow='ellipsis', get_renderable=None)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/live.rst

Creates a Live instance to render an auto-updating live display of a given renderable. Configure console, screen mode, refresh rate, and output redirection.

```APIDOC
## Live(renderable=None, console=None, screen=False, auto_refresh=True, refresh_per_second=4, transient=False, redirect_stdout=True, redirect_stderr=True, vertical_overflow='ellipsis', get_renderable=None)

### Description
Creates a Live instance to render an auto-updating live display of a given renderable. Configure console, screen mode, refresh rate, and output redirection.

### Parameters
- **renderable** (RenderableType, optional) - Optional - The renderable to live display. Defaults to displaying nothing.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional - Optional Console instance. Defaults to an internal Console instance writing to stdout.
- **screen** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable alternate screen mode. Defaults to False.
- **auto_refresh** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable auto refresh. If disabled, you will need to call refresh() or update() with refresh flag. Defaults to True.
- **refresh_per_second** (float, optional) - Optional - Number of times per second to refresh the live display. Defaults to 4.
- **transient** (bool, optional) - Optional - Clear the renderable on exit (has no effect when screen=True). Defaults to False.
- **redirect_stdout** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable redirection of stdout, so `print` may be used. Defaults to True.
- **redirect_stderr** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable redirection of stderr. Defaults to True.
- **vertical_overflow** (VerticalOverflowMethod, optional) - Optional - How to handle renderable when it is too tall for the console. Defaults to "ellipsis".
- **get_renderable** (Callable[[], RenderableType], optional) - Optional - Optional callable to get renderable. Defaults to None.
```

--------------------------------

### Progress class with context manager

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use Progress as a context manager to automatically start/stop the display. Add tasks with add_task() and update using advance. The loop continues until all tasks are finished.

```python
import time

from rich.progress import Progress

with Progress() as progress:

    task1 = progress.add_task("[red]Downloading...", total=1000)
    task2 = progress.add_task("[green]Processing...", total=1000)
    task3 = progress.add_task("[cyan]Cooking...", total=1000)

    while not progress.finished:
        progress.update(task1, advance=0.5)
        progress.update(task2, advance=0.3)
        progress.update(task3, advance=0.9)
        time.sleep(0.02)
```

Traverse object and generate a tree.

### Parameters
- **_object** (Any) - Required - Object to be traversed.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable truncating. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depth of data structures, or None for no maximum. Defaults to None.

### Returns
The root of a tree structure which can be used to render a pretty repr.

### Return Type
Node
```

### Bird class with standard __repr__

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

A class that might benefit from a Rich repr, with a standard __repr__ method and a dictionary of Bird instances.

```default
class Bird:
    def __init__(self, name, eats=None, fly=True, extinct=False):
        self.name = name
        self.eats = list(eats) if eats else []
        self.fly = fly
        self.extinct = extinct

    def __repr__(self):
        return f"Bird({self.name!r}, eats={self.eats!r}, fly={self.fly!r}, extinct={self.extinct!r})"

BIRDS = {
    "gull": Bird("gull", eats=["fish", "chips", "ice cream", "sausage rolls"]),
    "penguin": Bird("penguin", eats=["fish"], fly=False),
    "dodo": Bird("dodo", eats=["fruit"], fly=False, extinct=True)
}
print(BIRDS)
```

--------------------------------

### Implement __rich_repr__ with rich.repr.Result

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

Define a class with a manually implemented __rich_repr__ method that yields positional and keyword arguments. The method is annotated with rich.repr.Result to help catch logical errors. Use this when you need full control over the repr output.

```python
import rich.repr


class Bird:
    def __init__(self, name, eats=None, fly=True, extinct=False):
        self.name = name
        self.eats = list(eats) if eats else []
        self.fly = fly
        self.extinct = extinct

    def __rich_repr__(self) -> rich.repr.Result:
        yield self.name
        yield "eats", self.eats
        yield "fly", self.fly, True
        yield "extinct", self.extinct, False
```

### Defining and using a custom Theme

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/style.rst

Demonstrates creating a Theme instance and passing it to the Console constructor to use semantic style names.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.theme import Theme
custom_theme = Theme({
    "info": "dim cyan",
    "warning": "magenta",
    "danger": "bold red"
})
console = Console(theme=custom_theme)
console.print("This is information", style="info")
console.print("[warning]The pod bay doors are locked[/warning]")
console.print("Something terrible happened!", style="danger")
```

### from_data(data, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/json.rst

Class method that encodes a JSON object from arbitrary data and returns a new JSON renderable.

```APIDOC
## from_data(data, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)

### Description
Class method that encodes a JSON object from arbitrary data.

### Parameters
- **data** (Any) - Required - An object that may be encoded into JSON.
- **indent** (Union[None, int, str]) - Optional - Number of characters to indent by. Defaults to 2.
- **highlight** (bool) - Optional - Enable highlighting. Defaults to True.
- **skip_keys** (bool) - Optional - Skip keys not of a basic type. Defaults to False.
- **ensure_ascii** (bool) - Optional - Escape all non-ascii characters. Defaults to False.
- **check_circular** (bool) - Optional - Check for circular references. Defaults to True.
- **allow_nan** (bool) - Optional - Allow NaN and Infinity values. Defaults to True.
- **default** (Callable) - Optional - A callable that converts values that cannot be encoded into something that can be JSON encoded. Defaults to None.
- **sort_keys** (bool) - Optional - Sort dictionary keys. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **JSON** - New JSON object from the given data.

### Example
```python
from rich.json import JSON
json_renderable = JSON.from_data({'key': 'value'})
```
```

### Track progress with rich.progress.track

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Basic usage of the track function to display a progress bar while iterating over a sequence. Wrap any sequence in track and iterate over the result.

```python
from rich.progress import track

for step in track(range(100)):
    do_step(step)
```

### ConsoleOptions

```APIDOC
## ConsoleOptions

### Description
Options for the `__rich_console__` method, controlling how renderables are displayed.

### Constructor
`ConsoleOptions(size, legacy_windows, min_width, max_width, is_terminal, encoding, max_height, justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=False, highlight=None, markup=None, height=None)`

### Parameters

- **legacy_windows** (bool) - Required - Flag for legacy windows.
- **min_width** (int) - Required - Minimum width of renderable.
- **max_width** (int) - Required - Maximum width of renderable.
- **is_terminal** (bool) - Required - True if the target is a terminal, otherwise False.
- **encoding** (str) - Required - Encoding of terminal.
- **max_height** (int) - Required - Height of container (starts as terminal).
- **justify** (Literal['default', 'left', 'center', 'right', 'full'] | None) - Optional - Justify value override for renderable.
- **overflow** (Literal['fold', 'crop', 'ellipsis', 'ignore'] | None) - Optional - Overflow value override for renderable.
- **no_wrap** (bool | None) - Optional - Disable wrapping for text.
- **highlight** (bool | None) - Optional - Highlight override for render_str.
- **markup** (bool | None) - Optional - Enable markup when rendering strings.
- **height** (int | None) - Optional - Height of the renderable.

### Properties
- **ascii_only** (bool) - Check if renderables should use ascii only.
- **encoding** (str) - Encoding of terminal.
- **highlight** (bool | None) - Highlight override for render_str.
- **is_terminal** (bool) - True if the target is a terminal, otherwise False.
- **justify** (Literal['default', 'left', 'center', 'right', 'full'] | None) - Justify value override for renderable.
- **legacy_windows** (bool) - Flag for legacy windows.
- **markup** (bool | None) - Enable markup when rendering strings.
- **max_height** (int) - Height of container (starts as terminal).
- **max_width** (int) - Maximum width of renderable.
- **min_width** (int) - Minimum width of renderable.
- **no_wrap** (bool | None) - Disable wrapping for text.
- **overflow** (Literal['fold', 'crop', 'ellipsis', 'ignore'] | None) - Overflow value override for renderable.
- **size** (ConsoleDimensions) - Size of console.

### rich.console.Console

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Documentation for the rich.console.Console class, including its constructor parameters and methods.

```APIDOC
## Console Class

### Description
A high level console interface for rendering rich text, logging, and capturing output.

### Constructor
`Console(*, color_system='auto', force_terminal=None, force_jupyter=None, force_interactive=None, soft_wrap=False, theme=None, stderr=False, file=None, quiet=False, width=None, height=None, style=None, no_color=None, tab_size=8, record=False, markup=True, emoji=True, emoji_variant=None, highlight=True, log_time=True, log_path=True, log_time_format='[%X]', highlighter=<rich.highlighter.ReprHighlighter object>, legacy_windows=None, safe_box=True, get_datetime=None, get_time=None, _environ=None)`

### Parameters
- **color_system** (str, optional) - The color system supported by your terminal, either `"standard"`, `"256"` or `"truecolor"`. Leave as `"auto"` to autodetect.
- **force_terminal** (Optional[bool], optional) - Enable/disable terminal control codes, or None to auto-detect terminal. Defaults to None.
- **force_jupyter** (Optional[bool], optional) - Enable/disable Jupyter rendering, or None to auto-detect Jupyter. Defaults to None.
- **force_interactive** (Optional[bool], optional) - Enable/disable interactive mode, or None to auto detect. Defaults to None.
- **soft_wrap** (Optional[bool], optional) - Set soft wrap default on print method. Defaults to False.
- **theme** (Theme, optional) - An optional style theme object, or `None` for default theme.
- **stderr** (bool, optional) - Use stderr rather than stdout if `file` is not specified. Defaults to False.
- **file** (IO, optional) - A file object where the console should write to. Defaults to stdout.
- **quiet** (bool, Optional) - Boolean to suppress all output. Defaults to False.
- **width** (int, optional) - The width of the terminal. Leave as default to auto-detect width.
- **height** (int, optional) - The height of the terminal. Leave as default to auto-detect height.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Style to apply to all output, or None for no style. Defaults to None.
- **no_color** (Optional[bool], optional) - Enabled no color mode, or None to auto detect. Defaults to None.
- **tab_size** (int, optional) - Number of spaces used to replace a tab character. Defaults to 8.
- **record** (bool, optional) - Boolean to enable recording of terminal output, required to call `export_html()`, `export_svg()`, and `export_text()`. Defaults to False.
- **markup** (bool, optional) - Boolean to enable Console Markup. Defaults to True.
- **emoji** (bool, optional) - Enable emoji code. Defaults to True.
- **emoji_variant** (str, optional) - Optional emoji variant, either "text" or "emoji". Defaults to None.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Enable automatic highlighting. Defaults to True.
- **log_time** (bool, optional) - Boolean to enable logging of time by `log()` methods. Defaults to True.
- **log_path** (bool, optional) - Boolean to enable the logging of the caller by `log()`. Defaults to True.
- **log_time_format** (Union[str, TimeFormatterCallable], optional) - If `log_time` is enabled, either string for strftime or callable that formats the time. Defaults to "[%X] ".
- **highlighter** (HighlighterType, optional) - Default highlighter.
- **legacy_windows** (bool, optional) - Enable legacy Windows mode, or `None` to auto detect. Defaults to `None`.
- **safe_box** (bool, optional) - Restrict box options that don't render on legacy Windows.
- **get_datetime** (Callable[[], datetime], optional) - Callable that gets the current time as a datetime.datetime object (used by Console.log), or None for datetime.now.
- **get_time** (Callable[[], time], optional) - Callable that gets the current time in seconds, default uses time.monotonic.
- **_environ** (Mapping[str, str]) - Environment mapping.

### Methods

#### begin_capture()

Begin capturing console output. Call `end_capture()` to exit capture mode and return output.

**Return type:** None

#### bell()

Play a 'bell' sound (if supported by the terminal).

**Return type:** None

#### capture()

A context manager to *capture* the result of print() or log() in a string, rather than writing it to the console.

**Example:**
```pycon
>>> from rich.console import Console
>>> console = Console()
>>> with console.capture() as capture:
...     console.print("[bold magenta]Hello World[/]")
>>> print(capture.get())
```

**Returns:** Context manager with disables writing to the terminal.
**Return type:** Capture
```

## Style Class

### Description
Represents a terminal style with color, background color, and attributes like bold, italic, etc. Attributes can be on (True), off (False), or not set (None).

### Constructor

### Stylize Text with bold magenta

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Creates a Text instance and applies a style to a range of characters using the stylize method, then prints it to the console. The example prints 'Hello, World!' with the first word in bold magenta.

```default
from rich.console import Console
from rich.text import Text

console = Console()
text = Text("Hello, World!")
text.stylize("bold magenta", 0, 6)
console.print(text)
```

### rich.rule.Rule

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/rule.rst

Constructs a Rule renderable that draws a horizontal line with an optional title. The rule can be customized with characters for the line, style, end character, and title alignment.

```APIDOC
## rich.rule.Rule

### Description
Creates a Rule object, a console renderable that draws a horizontal rule (line). The rule can include a title and be customized in appearance.

### Constructor
`Rule(title='', characters='─', style='rule.line', end='\n', align='center')`

### Parameters
- **title** (Union[str, Text], optional) – Text to render in the rule. Defaults to “”.
- **characters** (str, optional) – Character(s) used to draw the line. Defaults to “─”.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) – Style of Rule. Defaults to “rule.line”.
- **end** (str, optional) – Character at end of Rule. Defaults to “\n”.
- **align** (str, optional) – How to align the title, one of “left”, “center”, or “right”. Defaults to “center”.
```

### styled (classmethod)

```APIDOC
## styled (classmethod)

### Description
Construct a Text instance with a pre-applied styled. A style applied in this way won't be used to pad the text when it is justified.

### Parameters
- **text** (str) - Required - A string containing console markup.
- **style** (Union[str, Style]) - Optional - Style to apply to the text. Defaults to ''.

### Progress

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Renders an auto-updating progress bar(s). This class is the main entry point for creating progress displays.

```APIDOC
## Progress

### Description
Renders an auto-updating progress bar(s).

### Constructor Parameters
- **console** (Console, optional) - Optional Console instance. Defaults to an internal Console instance writing to stdout.
- **auto_refresh** (bool, optional) - Enable auto refresh. If disabled, you will need to call refresh().
- **refresh_per_second** (float, optional) - Number of times per second to refresh the progress information. Defaults to 10.
- **speed_estimate_period** (float) - Period (in seconds) used to calculate the speed estimate. Defaults to 30.
- **transient** (bool) - Clear the progress on exit. Defaults to False.
- **redirect_stdout** (bool) - Enable redirection of stdout, so `print` may be used. Defaults to True.
- **redirect_stderr** (bool) - Enable redirection of stderr. Defaults to True.
- **get_time** (Optional[GetTimeCallable]) - A callable that gets the current time, or None to use Console.get_time. Defaults to None.
- **disable** (bool, optional) - Disable progress display. Defaults to False.
- **expand** (bool, optional) - Expand tasks table to fit width. Defaults to False.
- **columns** (Union[str, ProgressColumn]) - Columns to display in the progress bar.

```

### Customizing default styles

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/style.rst

Shows how to override built-in Rich styles by including them in a custom Theme.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.theme import Theme
console = Console(theme=Theme({"repr.number": "bold green blink"}))
console.print("The total is 128")
```

### export_html(, theme=None, clear=True, code_format=None, inline_styles=False)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Generates HTML from console contents. Requires record=True argument in constructor.

```APIDOC
## export_html(, theme=None, clear=True, code_format=None, inline_styles=False)

### Description
Generates HTML from console contents. Requires record=True argument in constructor.

### Method
export_html

### Parameters
- **theme** (TerminalTheme, optional) - TerminalTheme object containing console colors.
- **clear** (bool, optional) - Clear record buffer after exporting. Defaults to True.
- **code_format** (str, optional) - Format string to render HTML. In addition to '{foreground}', '{background}', and '{code}', should contain '{stylesheet}' if inline_styles is False.
- **inline_styles** (bool, optional) - If True styles will be inlined in to spans, which makes files larger but easier to cut and paste markup. If False, styles will be embedded in a style tag. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **str** - String containing console contents as HTML.
```

log.error("[bold red blink]Server is shutting down![/]", extra={"markup": True})

The Status class displays a status indicator with a 'spinner' animation. It is initialized with a status renderable and optional console, spinner, style, speed, and refresh rate parameters.

## Status

### Description
Displays a status indicator with a 'spinner' animation.

### Constructor Parameters
- **status** (RenderableType) - Required - A status renderable (str or Text typically).
- **console** (Console, optional) - Console instance to use, or None for global console. Defaults to None.

### rich.pretty.Node

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

A node in a repr tree, representing an atomic value or a container. Provides methods to check length constraints, iterate tokens, and render the node to a pretty repr string.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.Node

### Description
A node in a repr tree. May be atomic or a container.

### Constructor Parameters
- **key_repr** (str) - Required - The key representation.
- **value_repr** (str) - Required - The value representation.
- **open_brace** (str) - Required - The opening brace string.
- **close_brace** (str) - Required - The closing brace string.
- **empty** (str) - Required - The empty representation.
- **last** (bool) - Required - Whether this is the last node.
- **is_tuple** (bool) - Required - Whether this node represents a tuple.
- **is_namedtuple** (bool) - Required - Whether this node represents a namedtuple.
- **children** (List[Node] | None) - Required - The child nodes.
- **key_separator** (str) - Required - The separator between key and value.
- **separator** (str) - Required - The separator between items.

### Methods

#### check_length(start_length, max_length)

Check the length fits within a limit.

**Parameters:**
- **start_length** (int) - Required - Starting length of the line (indent, prefix, suffix).
- **max_length** (int) - Required - Maximum length.

**Returns:**
- **bool** - True if the node can be rendered within max length, otherwise False.

#### iter_tokens()

Generate tokens for this node.

**Returns:**
- **Iterable[str]** - An iterable of string tokens.

#### render(max_width=80, indent_size=4, expand_all=False)

Render the node to a pretty repr.

**Parameters:**
- **max_width** (int, optional) - Maximum width of the repr. Defaults to 80.
- **indent_size** (int, optional) - Size of indents. Defaults to 4.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Expand all levels. Defaults to False.

**Returns:**
- **str** - A repr string of the original object.
```

--------------------------------

### Custom RainbowHighlighter with Highlighter

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/highlighting.rst

Extends the Highlighter base class to implement a custom highlighting scheme. The highlight method is passed a Text object and stylizes each character with a random color from 16 to 255. The highlighter is used as a callable to print the highlighted text.

```python
from random import randint

from rich import print
from rich.highlighter import Highlighter


class RainbowHighlighter(Highlighter):
    def highlight(self, text):
        for index in range(len(text)):
            text.stylize(f"color({randint(16, 255)})", index, index + 1)


rainbow = RainbowHighlighter()
print(rainbow("I must not fear. Fear is the mind-killer."))
```

--------------------------------

### ProgressBar

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress_bar.rst

Documentation for the ProgressBar class, including its constructor, properties, and update method.

```APIDOC
## ProgressBar

### Description
Renders a progress bar, used by rich.progress. It can display a determinate progress bar with a total and completed count, or an indeterminate pulsing animation when total is None.

### Constructor
`ProgressBar(total=100.0, completed=0, width=None, pulse=False, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', animation_time=None)`

### Parameters
- **total** (float, optional) - Number of steps in the bar. Defaults to 100. Set to None to render a pulsing animation.
- **completed** (float, optional) - Number of steps completed. Defaults to 0.
- **width** (int, optional) - Width of the bar, or None for maximum width. Defaults to None.
- **pulse** (bool, optional) - Enable pulse effect. Defaults to False. Will pulse if a None total was passed.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the bar background. Defaults to "bar.back".
- **complete_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the completed bar. Defaults to "bar.complete".
- **finished_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for a finished bar. Defaults to "bar.finished".
- **pulse_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for pulsing bars. Defaults to "bar.pulse".
- **animation_time** (Optional[float], optional) - Time in seconds to use for animation, or None to use system time.

### Properties
- **percentage_completed** (float | None) - Calculate percentage complete.

### Methods
#### update(completed, total=None)
Update progress with new values.

**Parameters:**
- **completed** (float) - Number of steps completed.
- **total** (float, optional) - Total number of steps, or None to not change. Defaults to None.

**Return type:** None
```

--------------------------------

### Append styled text to Text

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Constructs a Text instance by appending strings with styles using the append method. The example appends 'Hello' with bold magenta style and ' World!' without style, then prints the result.

```default
text = Text()
text.append("Hello", style="bold magenta")
text.append(" World!")
console.print(text)
```

### rich.pretty.pretty_repr

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/pretty.rst

Function to generate a prettified repr string of an object, expanding it to multiple lines to fit within a specified width.

```APIDOC
## rich.pretty.pretty_repr(_object, *, max_width=80, indent_size=4, max_length=None, max_string=None, max_depth=None, expand_all=False)

### Description
Prettify repr string by expanding on to new lines to fit within a given width.

### Parameters
- **_object** (Any) - Required - Object to repr.
- **max_width** (int, optional) - Optional - Desired maximum width of repr string. Defaults to 80.
- **indent_size** (int, optional) - Optional - Number of spaces to indent. Defaults to 4.
- **max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to None.
- **max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable truncating. Defaults to None.
- **max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depth of nested data structure, or None for no depth. Defaults to None.
- **expand_all** (bool, optional) - Optional - Expand all containers regardless of available width. Defaults to False.

### Returns
A possibly multi-line representation of the object.

### Return Type
str
```

`filter_control(segments, is_control=False)`

- **segments** (Iterable[Segment]) - Required - An iterable of Segment instances.
- **is_control** (bool, optional) - Optional - is_control flag to match in search.

### Returns
- **Iterable[Segment]** - And iterable of Segment instances.

### Return type
Iterable[Segment]

```

### rich.print()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/init.rst

Prints objects to the terminal with rich formatting, mirroring the built-in print signature.

```APIDOC
## rich.print(*objects, sep=' ', end='\n', file=None, flush=False)

### Description
Print object(s) supplied via positional arguments. This function has an identical signature to the built-in print. For more advanced features, see the Console class.

### Method
Function

### Signature
`rich.print(*objects, sep=' ', end='\n', file=None, flush=False)`

### Parameters
- **objects** (Any) - Required - Objects to print.
- **sep** (str, optional) - Separator between printed objects. Defaults to " ".
- **end** (str, optional) - Character to write at end of output. Defaults to "\n".
- **file** (IO[str], optional) - File to write to, or None for stdout. Defaults to None.
- **flush** (bool, optional) - Has no effect as Rich always flushes output. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **None**
```

### rich.text.Text

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/text.rst

Documentation for the rich.text.Text class and its methods.

```APIDOC
## Text Class

### Description
Represents text with color and style, providing methods for alignment, appending, metadata, and more.

### Constructor
`Text(text='', style='', justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=None, end='\n', tab_size=None, spans=None)`

#### Parameters
- **text** (str, optional) - Default unstyled text. Defaults to "".
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Base style for text. Defaults to "".
- **justify** (str, optional) - Justify method: "left", "center", "full", "right". Defaults to None.
- **overflow** (str, optional) - Overflow method: "crop", "fold", "ellipsis". Defaults to None.
- **no_wrap** (bool, optional) - Disable text wrapping, or None for default. Defaults to None.
- **end** (str, optional) - Character to end text with. Defaults to "\n".
- **tab_size** (int) - Number of spaces per tab, or `None` to use `console.tab_size`. Defaults to None.
- **spans** (List[Span], optional) - List of spans.

### Methods

#### align(align, width, character=' ')
Align text to a given width.

- **Parameters:**
  - **align** (AlignMethod) - One of "left", "center", or "right".
  - **width** (int) - Desired width.
  - **character** (str, optional) - Character to pad with. Defaults to " ".
- **Return type:** None

#### append(text, style=None)
Add text with an optional style.

- **Parameters:**
  - **text** (Union[Text, str]) - A str or Text to append.
  - **style** (str, optional) - A style name. Defaults to None.
- **Returns:** Returns self for chaining.
- **Return type:** Text

#### append_text(text)
Append another Text instance. This method is more performant than Text.append, but only works for Text.

- **Parameters:**
  - **text** (Text) - The Text instance to append to this instance.
- **Returns:** Returns self for chaining.
- **Return type:** Text

#### append_tokens(tokens)
Append iterable of str and style. Style may be a Style instance or a str style definition.

- **Parameters:**
  - **tokens** (Iterable[Tuple[str, Optional[StyleType]]]) - An iterable of tuples containing str content and style.
- **Returns:** Returns self for chaining.
- **Return type:** Text

#### apply_meta(meta, start=0, end=None)
Apply metadata to the text, or a portion of the text.

- **Parameters:**
  - **meta** (Dict[str, Any]) - A dict of meta information.
  - **start** (int) - Start offset (negative indexing is supported). Defaults to 0.
  - **end** (Optional[int], optional) - End offset (negative indexing is supported), or None for end of text. Defaults to None.
- **Return type:** None

#### assemble(\*parts, style='', justify=None, overflow=None, no_wrap=None, end='\n', tab_size=8, meta=None)
Construct a text instance by combining a sequence of strings with optional styles. The positional arguments should be either strings, or a tuple of string + style.

- **Parameters:**
  - **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Base style for text. Defaults to "".
  - **justify** (str, optional) - Justify method: "left", "center", "full", "right". Defaults to None.
  - **overflow** (str, optional) - Overflow method: "crop", "fold", "ellipsis". Defaults to None.
  - **no_wrap** (bool, optional) - Disable text wrapping, or None for default. Defaults to None.
  - **end** (str, optional) - Character to end text with. Defaults to "\n".
  - **tab_size** (int) - Number of spaces per tab, or `None` to use `console.tab_size`. Defaults to None.
  - **meta** (Dict[str, Any], optional) - Metadata.
  - **parts** (str | Text | Tuple[str, str | Style]) - Parts to assemble.
- **Returns:** A new text instance.
- **Return type:** Text

#### blank_copy(plain='')
Return a new Text instance with copied metadata (but not the string or spans).

- **Parameters:**
  - **plain** (str) - Plain text.
- **Return type:** Text

#### cell_len
Get the number of cells required to render this text.

- **Return type:** int

#### copy()
Return a copy of this instance.

- **Return type:** Text

#### copy_styles(text)
Copy styles from another Text instance.

- **Parameters:**
  - **text** (Text) - A Text instance to copy styles from, must be the same length.
- **Return type:** None

#### detect_indentation()
Auto-detect indentation of code.

- **Returns:** Number of spaces used to indent code.
- **Return type:** int

#### divide(offsets)
Divide text into a number of lines at given offsets.

- **Parameters:**
  - **offsets** (Iterable[int]) - Offsets used to divide text.
- **Returns:** New RichText instances between offsets.
- **Return type:** Lines
```

### Check if object is RichRenderable with isinstance

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/abc.rst

Use isinstance to verify that an object supports the Rich renderable protocol before printing it with console.print. This is the intended use of the RichRenderable base class.

```python
if isinstance(my_object, RichRenderable):
    console.print(my_object)
```

### Add __rich_repr__ method

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

Add this __rich_repr__ method to the Bird class to enable Rich formatting. It yields tuples that specify positional and keyword arguments, with defaults omitted.

```default
def __rich_repr__(self):
    yield self.name
    yield "eats", self.eats
    yield "fly", self.fly, True
    yield "extinct", self.extinct, False
```

### Align a panel to the center

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/align.rst

Creates a 20-character-wide panel and renders it centered in the terminal using Align with align="center". Requires importing Console, Align, and Panel.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.align import Align
from rich.panel import Panel

console = Console()
# Create a panel 20 characters wide
p = Panel("Hello, [b]World[/b]!", style="on green", width=20)

# Renders the panel centered in the terminal
console.print(Align(p, align="center"))
```

### Syntax highlighting with Syntax object

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/syntax.rst

Construct a Syntax object from a string and print it to the console. The example reads a Python file and highlights it as Python.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.syntax import Syntax

console = Console()
with open("syntax.py", "rt") as code_file:
    syntax = Syntax(code_file.read(), "python")
console.print(syntax)
```

### track(sequence, total=None, completed=0, task_id=None, description='Working...', update_period=0.1)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Track progress by iterating over a sequence. You can also track progress of an iterable, which might require that you additionally specify `total`.

```APIDOC
## track(sequence, total=None, completed=0, task_id=None, description='Working...', update_period=0.1)

### Description
Track progress by iterating over a sequence. You can also track progress of an iterable, which might require that you additionally specify `total`.

### Parameters
- **sequence** (Iterable[ProgressType]) - Required - Values you want to iterate over and track progress.
- **total** (float | None) - Optional - Total number of steps. Default is len(sequence).
- **completed** (int, optional) - Number of steps completed so far. Defaults to 0.
- **task_id** (TaskID | None) - Optional - Task to track. Default is new task.
- **description** (str) - Optional - Description of task, if new task is created.
- **update_period** (float, optional) - Minimum time (in seconds) between calls to update(). Defaults to 0.1.

### Returns
Iterable[ProgressType] - An iterable of values taken from the provided sequence.
```

### Handle exceptions with rich_tracebacks

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/logging.rst

Configures RichHandler with rich_tracebacks=True to format exceptions using Rich's Traceback class, providing more context. The example logs an exception from a division by zero.

```python
import logging
from rich.logging import RichHandler

logging.basicConfig(
    level="NOTSET",
    format="%(message)s",
    datefmt="[%X]",
    handlers=[RichHandler(rich_tracebacks=True)]
)

log = logging.getLogger("rich")
try:
    print(1 / 0)
except Exception:
    log.exception("unable print!")
```

### Export SVG with MONOKAI theme

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Export console output to an SVG file with a custom theme. Import the desired theme from rich.terminal_theme and pass it to save_svg() via the theme parameter. The SVG width matches the terminal width in characters, and the height scales automatically.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.terminal_theme import MONOKAI

console = Console(record=True)
console.save_svg("example.svg", theme=MONOKAI)
```

### update(task_id, total=None, completed=None, advance=None, description=None, visible=None, refresh=False, **fields)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Update information associated with a task.

```APIDOC
## update(task_id, total=None, completed=None, advance=None, description=None, visible=None, refresh=False, **fields)

### Description
Update information associated with a task.

### Parameters
- **task_id** (TaskID) - Required - Task id (returned by add_task).
- **total** (float, optional) - Updates task.total if not None.
- **completed** (float, optional) - Updates task.completed if not None.
- **advance** (float, optional) - Add a value to task.completed if not None.
- **description** (str, optional) - Change task description if not None.
- **visible** (bool, optional) - Set visible flag if not None.
- **refresh** (bool) - Force a refresh of progress information. Default is False.
- **\*\*fields** (Any) - Additional data fields required for rendering.

### Returns
None
```

--------------------------------

### Basic usage of rich.progress.track

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use track() to iterate over a sequence while displaying a progress bar. The description parameter labels the task. Each iteration updates the progress; time.sleep simulates work.

```default
import time
from rich.progress import track

for i in track(range(20), description="Processing..."):
    time.sleep(1)  # Simulate work being done
```

--------------------------------

### reset(task_id, start=True, total=None, completed=0, visible=None, description=None, **fields)

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Resets a task so completed is 0 and the clock is reset.

```APIDOC
## reset(task_id, start=True, total=None, completed=0, visible=None, description=None, **fields)

### Description
Resets a task so completed is 0 and the clock is reset.

### Parameters
- **task_id** (TaskID) - Required - ID of task.
- **start** (bool, optional) - Start the task after reset. Defaults to True.
- **total** (float, optional) - New total steps in task, or None to use current total. Defaults to None.
- **completed** (int, optional) - Number of steps completed. Defaults to 0.
- **visible** (bool, optional) - Set visible flag if not None.
- **description** (str, optional) - Change task description if not None. Defaults to None.
- **\*\*fields** (str) - Additional data fields required for rendering.

### Returns
None
```

### Console.out

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Low-level method to write to the terminal without pretty printing, wrapping, or markup.

```APIDOC
## Console.out

### Description
Output to the terminal. This is a low-level way of writing to the terminal which unlike `print()` won't pretty print, wrap text, or apply markup, but will optionally apply highlighting and a basic style.

### Method
out

### Signature
`out(*objects, sep=' ', end='\n', style=None, highlight=None)`

### Parameters
- **objects** (*Any*) - Required - Objects to output.
- **sep** (*str*, optional) - String to write between print data. Defaults to " ".
- **end** (*str*, optional) - String to write at end of print data. Defaults to "\n".
- **style** (*Union[str, Style]*, optional) - A style to apply to output. Defaults to None.
- **highlight** (*Optional[bool]*, optional) - Enable automatic highlighting, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to `None`.

### Return Type
None
```

### rich.print_json()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/init.rst

Pretty prints JSON data or a JSON string with syntax highlighting and configurable formatting.

```APIDOC
## rich.print_json(json=None, data=None, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)

### Description
Pretty prints JSON. Output will be valid JSON.

### Method
Function

### Signature
`rich.print_json(json=None, data=None, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)`

### Parameters
- **json** (str) - Optional - A string containing JSON.
- **data** (Any) - Optional - If json is not supplied, then encode this data.
- **indent** (int, optional) - Number of spaces to indent. Defaults to 2.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Enable highlighting of output. Defaults to True.
- **skip_keys** (bool, optional) - Skip keys not of a basic type. Defaults to False.
- **ensure_ascii** (bool, optional) - Escape all non-ascii characters. Defaults to False.
- **check_circular** (bool, optional) - Check for circular references. Defaults to True.
- **allow_nan** (bool, optional) - Allow NaN and Infinity values. Defaults to True.
- **default** (Callable, optional) - A callable that converts values that can not be encoded in to something that can be JSON encoded. Defaults to None.
- **sort_keys** (bool, optional) - Sort dictionary keys. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **None**
```

### rich.inspect()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/init.rst

Inspects any Python object and displays summarized information, with options to show methods, help, private attributes, and more.

```APIDOC
## rich.inspect(obj, console=None, title=None, help=False, methods=False, docs=True, private=False, dunder=False, sort=True, all=False, value=True)

### Description
Inspect any Python object. Provides summarized info by default, with options to show methods, full help, private attributes, dunder attributes, or all attributes.

### Method
Function

### Signature
`rich.inspect(obj, console=None, title=None, help=False, methods=False, docs=True, private=False, dunder=False, sort=True, all=False, value=True)`

### Parameters
- **obj** (Any) - Required - An object to inspect.
- **title** (str, optional) - Title to display over inspect result, or None use type. Defaults to None.
- **help** (bool, optional) - Show full help text rather than just first paragraph. Defaults to False.
- **methods** (bool, optional) - Enable inspection of callables. Defaults to False.
- **docs** (bool, optional) - Also render doc strings. Defaults to True.
- **private** (bool, optional) - Show private attributes (beginning with underscore). Defaults to False.
- **dunder** (bool, optional) - Show attributes starting with double underscore. Defaults to False.
- **sort** (bool, optional) - Sort attributes alphabetically, callables at the top, leading and trailing underscores ignored. Defaults to True.
- **all** (bool, optional) - Show all attributes. Defaults to False.
- **value** (bool, optional) - Pretty print value. Defaults to True.
- **console** (Console | None) - Optional console to use.

### Returns
- **None**
```

--------------------------------

### Inspect a Python object with Rich

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/introduction.rst

Uses Rich's `inspect()` function to generate a report on a Python object, including its methods. This is a debug aid and shows the output Rich can generate.

```python
>>> from rich import inspect
>>> from rich.color import Color
>>> color = Color.parse("red")
>>> inspect(color, methods=True)
```

### save_html

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Generates HTML from console contents and writes it to a file. Requires `record=True` argument in constructor.

```APIDOC
## save_html(path, theme=None, clear=True, code_format='<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html>\n<head>\n<meta charset="UTF-8">\n<style>\n{stylesheet}\nbody {{\n    color: {foreground};\n    background-color: {background};\n}}\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n    <pre style="font-family:Menlo,\'DejaVu Sans Mono\',consolas,\'Courier New\',monospace"><code style="font-family:inherit">{code}</code></pre>\n</body>\n</html>\n', inline_styles=False)

### Description
Generates HTML from console contents and writes it to a file. Requires `record=True` argument in constructor.

### Parameters
- **path** (Union[str, PathLike[str]]) - Required - Path to write html file.
- **theme** (TerminalTheme, optional) - TerminalTheme object containing console colors.
- **clear** (bool, optional) - Clear record buffer after exporting. Defaults to `True`.
- **code_format** (str, optional) - Format string to render HTML. In addition to '{foreground}', '{background}', and '{code}', should contain '{stylesheet}' if inline_styles is `False`.
- **inline_styles** (bool, optional) - If `True` styles will be inlined in to spans, which makes files larger but easier to cut and paste markup. If `False`, styles will be embedded in a style tag. Defaults to False.

### Returns
- **None** - No return value.
```

### Convert ANSI formatted text to Text using from_ansi

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/text.rst

Converts a string with ANSI escape codes into a Text object using the from_ansi method, then prints the spans of the Text. The ANSI code [1;35mHello[0m applies bold magenta to 'Hello'.

```default
text = Text.from_ansi("\033[1;35mHello\033[0m, World!")
console.print(text.spans)
```

--------------------------------

### Print recursive error with max_frames

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/traceback.rst

Print a recursive error traceback with max_frames=20. The max_frames argument defaults to 100; if a traceback exceeds that, only the first 50 and last 50 frames are shown. Set max_frames to 0 to disable this feature.

```default
from rich.console import Console


def foo(n):
    return bar(n)


def bar(n):
    return foo(n)


console = Console()

try:
    foo(1)
except Exception:
    console.print_exception(max_frames=20)
```

--------------------------------

### Draw a rule with console.rule

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Draws a horizontal line with an optional title. The example shows a red bold title. The method also accepts style and align parameters.

```pycon
>>> console.rule("[bold red]Chapter 2")
```

### Rendering Markdown with Rich: Python example using Markdown class

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/markdown.rst

Construct a Markdown object with markdown text and print it to a Console. This snippet shows a minimal example with a markdown string that includes an h1, italic text, and a list. Note that code blocks are rendered with full syntax highlighting.

```python
MARKDOWN = """
# This is an h1

Rich can do a pretty *decent* job of rendering markdown.

1. This is a list item
2. This is another list item
"""
from rich.console import Console
from rich.markdown import Markdown

console = Console()
md = Markdown(MARKDOWN)
console.print(md)

```

### rich.progress.track

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Function to track progress by iterating over a sequence.

```APIDOC
## rich.progress.track(sequence, description='Working...', total=None, completed=0, auto_refresh=True, console=None, transient=False, get_time=None, refresh_per_second=10, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', update_period=0.1, disable=False, show_speed=True)

### Description
Track progress by iterating over a sequence. You can also track progress of an iterable, which might require that you additionally specify `total`.

### Parameters
- **sequence** (Iterable[ProgressType]) - Required - Values you wish to iterate over and track progress.
- **description** (str, optional) - Description of task show next to progress bar. Defaults to "Working".
- **total** (Optional[float]) - Total number of steps. Default is len(sequence).
- **completed** (int, optional) - Number of steps completed so far. Defaults to 0.
- **auto_refresh** (bool, optional) - Automatic refresh, disable to force a refresh after each iteration. Default is True.
- **transient** (bool) - Clear the progress on exit. Defaults to False.
- **console** (Console, optional) - Console to write to. Default creates internal Console instance.
- **refresh_per_second** (float) - Number of times per second to refresh the progress information. Defaults to 10.
- **style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the bar background. Defaults to "bar.back".
- **complete_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for the completed bar. Defaults to "bar.complete".
- **finished_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for a finished bar. Defaults to "bar.finished".
- **pulse_style** (StyleType, optional) - Style for pulsing bars. Defaults to "bar.pulse".
- **update_period** (float, optional) - Minimum time (in seconds) between calls to update(). Defaults to 0.1.
- **disable** (bool, optional) - Disable display of progress.
- **show_speed** (bool, optional) - Show speed if total isn't known. Defaults to True.
- **get_time** (Optional[Callable[[], float]]) - Optional callable to get the current time.

### Returns
An iterable of the values in the sequence.

### Return Type
Iterable[ProgressType]
```

--------------------------------

### Console.log

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Logs rich content to the terminal with configurable separators, styles, and highlighting.

```APIDOC
## Console.log(*objects, sep=' ', end='\n', style=None, justify=None, emoji=None, markup=None, highlight=None, log_locals=False, _stack_offset=1)

### Description
Log rich content to the terminal.

### Parameters
- **objects** (positional args) - Required - Objects to log to the terminal.
- **sep** (str, optional) - Optional - String to write between print data. Defaults to " ".
- **end** (str, optional) - Optional - String to write at end of print data. Defaults to "\n".
- **style** (Union[str, Style], optional) - Optional - A style to apply to output. Defaults to None.
- **justify** (str, optional) - Optional - One of "left", "right", "center", or "full". Defaults to `None`.
- **emoji** (Optional[bool], optional) - Optional - Enable emoji code, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to None.
- **markup** (Optional[bool], optional) - Optional - Enable markup, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to None.
- **highlight** (Optional[bool], optional) - Optional - Enable automatic highlighting, or `None` to use console default. Defaults to None.
- **log_locals** (bool, optional) - Optional - Boolean to enable logging of locals where `log()` was called. Defaults to False.
- **_stack_offset** (int, optional) - Optional - Offset of caller from end of call stack. Defaults to 1.

### Return Type
None
```

### Set up RichHandler for logging

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/logging.rst

Sets up a RichHandler as the logging handler for Python's logging module, formatting and colorizing log output. The example configures basicConfig with the handler and logs a simple message.

```python
import logging
from rich.logging import RichHandler

FORMAT = "%(message)s"
logging.basicConfig(
    level="NOTSET", format=FORMAT, datefmt="[%X]", handlers=[RichHandler()]
)

log = logging.getLogger("rich")
log.info("Hello, World!")
```

### Display content on alternate screen with console.screen()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/console.rst

Use the screen context manager to display content on the alternate screen. The alternate screen is separate from the regular terminal and leaves your command history intact. The example prints a dictionary and returns to the command prompt after 5 seconds.

```python
from time import sleep
from rich.console import Console

console = Console()
with console.screen():
    console.print(locals())
    sleep(5)
```

### rich.traceback.install

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/traceback.rst

Function to install a rich traceback handler globally.

```APIDOC
## rich.traceback.install(console=None, width=100, code_width=88, extra_lines=3, theme=None, word_wrap=False, show_locals=False, locals_max_length=10, locals_max_string=80, locals_max_depth=None, locals_hide_dunder=True, locals_hide_sunder=None, locals_overflow=None, indent_guides=True, suppress=(), max_frames=100)

### Description
Installs a rich traceback handler. Once installed, any tracebacks will be printed with syntax highlighting and rich formatting.

### Parameters
- **console** (Optional[Console], optional) - Optional - Console to write exception to. Default uses internal Console instance.
- **width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Width (in characters) of traceback. Defaults to 100.
- **code_width** (Optional[int], optional) - Optional - Code width (in characters) of traceback. Defaults to 88.
- **extra_lines** (int, optional) - Optional - Extra lines of code. Defaults to 3.
- **theme** (Optional[str], optional) - Optional - Pygments theme to use in traceback. Defaults to `None` which will pick a theme appropriate for the platform.
- **word_wrap** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable word wrapping of long lines. Defaults to False.
- **show_locals** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable display of local variables. Defaults to False.
- **locals_max_length** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of containers before abbreviating, or None for no abbreviation. Defaults to 10.
- **locals_max_string** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum length of string before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to 80.
- **locals_max_depth** (int, optional) - Optional - Maximum depths of locals before truncating, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **locals_hide_dunder** (bool, optional) - Optional - Hide locals prefixed with double underscore. Defaults to True.
- **locals_hide_sunder** (bool, optional) - Optional - Hide locals prefixed with single underscore. Defaults to False.
- **locals_overflow** (OverflowMethod, optional) - Optional - How to handle overflowing locals, or None to disable. Defaults to None.
- **indent_guides** (bool, optional) - Optional - Enable indent guides in code and locals. Defaults to True.
- **suppress** (Sequence[Union[str, ModuleType]]) - Optional - Optional sequence of modules or paths to exclude from traceback.
- **max_frames** (int) - Optional - Maximum number of frames to show in a traceback, 0 for no maximum. Defaults to 100.

### Returns
The previous exception handler that was replaced.

### Return Type
Callable
```

--------------------------------

### Display a status spinner with Console.status

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/README.md

Use Console.status to display a spinner animation and message while performing tasks. The animation does not block console usage. The example shows a loop that pops tasks from a list, sleeps one second per task, and logs completion messages.

```python
from time import sleep
from rich.console import Console

console = Console()
tasks = [f"task {n}" for n in range(1, 11)]

with console.status("[bold green]Working on tasks...") as status:
    while tasks:
        task = tasks.pop(0)
        sleep(1)
        console.log(f"{task} complete")
```

--------------------------------

### Syntax highlighting with from_path()

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/syntax.rst

Use the from_path() alternative constructor to load code from disk and auto-detect the file type. This is a more concise way to highlight a file.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.syntax import Syntax

console = Console()
syntax = Syntax.from_path("syntax.py")
console.print(syntax)
```

### Print/log above progress with progress.console

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use Progress() to create a console accessible via progress.console to print output above the progress display. Example adds a task and iterates to print job messages and advance the progress.

```python
with Progress() as progress:
    task = progress.add_task("twiddling thumbs", total=10)
    for job in range(10):
        progress.console.print(f"Working on job #{job}")
        run_job(job)
        progress.advance(task)
```

### print_json

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Pretty prints JSON. Output will be valid JSON.

```APIDOC
## print_json(json=None, data=None, indent=2, highlight=True, skip_keys=False, ensure_ascii=False, check_circular=True, allow_nan=True, default=None, sort_keys=False)

### Description
Pretty prints JSON. Output will be valid JSON.

### Parameters
- **json** (Optional[str]) - A string containing JSON.
- **data** (Any) - If json is not supplied, then encode this data.
- **indent** (Union[None, int, str], optional) - Number of spaces to indent. Defaults to 2.
- **highlight** (bool, optional) - Enable highlighting of output. Defaults to True.
- **skip_keys** (bool, optional) - Skip keys not of a basic type. Defaults to False.
- **ensure_ascii** (bool, optional) - Escape all non-ascii characters. Defaults to False.
- **check_circular** (bool, optional) - Check for circular references. Defaults to True.
- **allow_nan** (bool, optional) - Allow NaN and Infinity values. Defaults to True.
- **default** (Callable, optional) - A callable that converts values that can not be encoded in to something that can be JSON encoded. Defaults to None.
- **sort_keys** (bool, optional) - Sort dictionary keys. Defaults to False.

### Returns
None
```

--------------------------------

### Use group decorator to build group from generator

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/group.rst

Use the group() decorator to build a Group from a generator function that yields renderables. This is equivalent to the previous Group class example but useful when the number of renderables is dynamic or larger.

```python
from rich import print
from rich.console import group
from rich.panel import Panel

@group()
def get_panels():
    yield Panel("Hello", style="on blue")
    yield Panel("World", style="on red")

print(Panel(get_panels()))
```

>>> from rich.prompt import Prompt
>>> name = Prompt.ask("Enter your name", choices=["Paul", "Jessica", "Duncan"], default="Paul", case_sensitive=False)

--------------------------------

### Progress without context manager with start/stop

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

When not using the context manager, call start() before adding tasks and stop() in a finally block to ensure proper cleanup. Uses try/finally to guarantee stop() is called.

```python
import time

from rich.progress import Progress

progress = Progress()
progress.start()
try:
    task1 = progress.add_task("[red]Downloading...", total=1000)
    task2 = progress.add_task("[green]Processing...", total=1000)
    task3 = progress.add_task("[cyan]Cooking...", total=1000)

    while not progress.finished:
        progress.update(task1, advance=0.5)
        progress.update(task2, advance=0.3)
        progress.update(task3, advance=0.9)
        time.sleep(0.02)
finally:
    progress.stop()
```

### Prompt.ask

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/prompt.rst

Prompts the user for input with optional parameters for password, choices, and default values.

```APIDOC
## Prompt.ask

### Description
Prompts the user for input with optional parameters for password, choices, and default values.

### Method
ask

### Parameters
- **prompt** (TextType, optional) - Prompt text. Defaults to "".
- **console** (Console, optional) - A Console instance or None to use global console. Defaults to None.
- **password** (bool, optional) - Enable password input. Defaults to False.
- **choices** (List[str], optional) - A list of valid choices. Defaults to None.
- **case_sensitive** (bool, optional) - Matching of choices should be case-sensitive. Defaults to True.
- **show_default** (bool, optional) - Show default in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **show_choices** (bool, optional) - Show choices in prompt. Defaults to True.
- **stream** (TextIO, optional) - Optional text file open for reading to get input. Defaults to None.

### Example
```pycon
>>> filename = Prompt.ask("Enter a filename")
```
```

### export_svg

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/console.rst

Exports the console contents as an SVG image. Requires record=True in the Console constructor.

```APIDOC
## export_svg(title='Rich', theme=None, clear=True, code_format='<svg class="rich-terminal" viewBox="0 0 {width} {height}" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">\n    <!-- Generated with Rich https://www.textualize.io -->\n    <style>\n\n    @font-face {{\n        font-family: "Fira Code";\n        src: local("FiraCode-Regular"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff2/FiraCode-Regular.woff2") format("woff2"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff/FiraCode-Regular.woff") format("woff");\n        font-style: normal;\n        font-weight: 400;\n    }}\n    @font-face {{\n        font-family: "Fira Code";\n        src: local("FiraCode-Bold"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff2/FiraCode-Bold.woff2") format("woff2"),\n                url("https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/firacode/6.2.0/woff/FiraCode-Bold.woff") format("woff");\n        font-style: bold;\n        font-weight: 700;\n    }}\n\n    .{unique_id}-matrix {{\n        font-family: Fira Code, monospace;\n        font-size: {char_height}px;\n        line-height: {line_height}px;\n        font-variant-east-asian: full-width;\n    }}\n\n    .{unique_id}-title {{\n        font-size: 18px;\n        font-weight: bold;\n        font-family: arial;\n    }}\n\n    {styles}\n    </style>\n\n    <defs>\n    <clipPath id="{unique_id}-clip-terminal">\n      <rect x="0" y="0" width="{terminal_width}" height="{terminal_height}" />\n    </clipPath>\n    {lines}\n    </defs>\n\n    {chrome}\n    <g transform="translate({terminal_x}, {terminal_y})" clip-path="url(#{unique_id}-clip-terminal)">\n    {backgrounds}\n    <g class="{unique_id}-matrix">\n    {matrix}\n    </g>\n    </g>\n</svg>\n', font_aspect_ratio=0.61, unique_id=None)

### Description
Generate an SVG from the console contents. Requires record=True in Console constructor.

### Method
Function call

### Endpoint
Console.export_svg()

### Parameters
- **title** (str, optional) - The title of the tab in the output image.
- **theme** (TerminalTheme, optional) - The TerminalTheme object to use to style the terminal.
- **clear** (bool, optional) - Clear record buffer after exporting. Defaults to True.
- **code_format** (str, optional) - Format string used to generate the SVG. Rich will inject a number of variables into the string in order to form the final SVG output. The default template used and the variables injected by Rich can be found by inspecting the console.CONSOLE_SVG_FORMAT variable.
- **font_aspect_ratio** (float, optional) - The width to height ratio of the font used in the code_format string. Defaults to 0.61, which is the width to height ratio of Fira Code (the default font). If you aren't specifying a different font inside code_format, you probably don't need this.
- **unique_id** (str, optional) - unique id that is used as the prefix for various elements (CSS styles, node ids). If not set, this defaults to a computed value based on the recorded content.

### Returns
- **str** - The SVG content as a string.

### Return Type
str
```

### Suppress framework frames with tracebacks_suppress

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/logging.rst

Suppresses framework frames (e.g., click) from Rich tracebacks by passing tracebacks_suppress=[click] to RichHandler. Suppressed frames show only the line and file, without code.

```python
import click
import logging
from rich.logging import RichHandler

logging.basicConfig(
    level="NOTSET",
    format="%(message)s",
    datefmt="[%X]",
    handlers=[RichHandler(rich_tracebacks=True, tracebacks_suppress=[click])]
)
```

### rich.progress.open

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/reference/progress.rst

Documentation for the rich.progress.open function, which reads bytes from a file while tracking progress.

```APIDOC
## rich.progress.open

### Description
Read bytes from a file while tracking progress. This function opens a file (or accepts a binary file-like object) and returns a context manager that yields a progress reader, displaying a progress bar during the read operation.

### Method
Function call

### Endpoint
`rich.progress.open(file, mode='rb', buffering=-1, encoding=None, errors=None, newline=None, total=None, description='Reading...', auto_refresh=True, console=None, transient=False, get_time=None, refresh_per_second=10, style='bar.back', complete_style='bar.complete', finished_style='bar.finished', pulse_style='bar.pulse', disable=False)`

### Parameters
- **file** (str | PathLike[str] | bytes) - Required - The path to the file to read, or a file-like object in binary mode.
- **mode** (Literal['rb']) - Required - The mode to use to open the file. Only supports "r", "rb" or "rt".
- **buffering** (int) - Optional - The buffering strategy to use, see `io.open()`. Defaults to -1.
- **encoding** (str | None) - Optional - The encoding to use when reading in text mode, see `io.open()`. Defaults to None.
- **errors** (str | None) - Optional - The error handling strategy for decoding errors, see `io.open()`. Defaults to None.
- **newline** (str | None) - Optional - The strategy for handling newlines in text mode, see `io.open()`. Defaults to None.
- **total** (int | None) - Optional - Total number of bytes to read. Must be provided if reading from a file handle. Default for a path is os.stat(file).st_size.
- **description** (str) - Optional - Description of task show next to progress bar. Defaults to "Reading".
- **auto_refresh** (bool) - Optional - Automatic refresh, disable to force a refresh after each iteration. Default is True.
- **console** (Console | None) - Optional - Console to write to. Default creates internal Console instance.
- **transient** (bool) - Optional - Clear the progress on exit. Defaults to False.
- **get_time** (Callable[[], float] | None) - Optional - A callable that returns the current time. Defaults to None.
- **refresh_per_second** (float) - Optional - Number of times per second to refresh the progress information. Defaults to 10.
- **style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for the bar background. Defaults to "bar.back".
- **complete_style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for the completed bar. Defaults to "bar.complete".
- **finished_style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for a finished bar. Defaults to "bar.finished".
- **pulse_style** (StyleType) - Optional - Style for pulsing bars. Defaults to "bar.pulse".
- **disable** (bool) - Optional - Disable display of progress. Defaults to False.

### Returns
A context manager yielding a progress reader.

### Return Type
ContextManager[BinaryIO]
```

--------------------------------

### Show progress while reading from a URL with wrap_file

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use rich.progress.wrap_file to wrap an existing file object and display a progress bar. You must set the expected number of bytes. Example reads a URL and prints its content line by line.

```python
from time import sleep
from urllib.request import urlopen

from rich.progress import wrap_file

response = urlopen("https://www.textualize.io")
size = int(response.headers["Content-Length"])

with wrap_file(response, size) as file:
    for line in file:
        print(line.decode("utf-8"), end="")
        sleep(0.1)
```

## Console.line(count=1)

### Auto-generate rich repr with @rich.repr.auto

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/pretty.rst

Use the @rich.repr.auto decorator to automatically generate a rich repr for a class whose __init__ parameters match attribute names. The decorator also creates a __repr__, so you get an auto-generated repr even without Rich printing. This example also prints a dictionary of Bird instances with rich.print.

```python
import rich.repr

@rich.repr.auto
class Bird:
    def __init__(self, name, eats=None, fly=True, extinct=False):
        self.name = name
        self.eats = list(eats) if eats else []
        self.fly = fly
        self.extinct = extinct


BIRDS = {
    "gull": Bird("gull", eats=["fish", "chips", "ice cream", "sausage rolls"]),
    "penguin": Bird("penguin", eats=["fish"], fly=False),
    "dodo": Bird("dodo", eats=["fruit"], fly=False, extinct=True)
}
from rich import print
print(BIRDS)
```

--------------------------------

### Show progress while reading a file with rich.progress.open

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/progress.rst

Use rich.progress.open to get a context manager that displays a progress bar while reading a file. Example reads a JSON file and prints the loaded data.

```python
import json
import rich.progress

with rich.progress.open("data.json", "rb") as file:
    data = json.load(file)
print(data)
```

### Custom EmailHighlighter with RegexHighlighter

Source: https://github.com/textualize/rich/blob/main/docs/source/highlighting.rst

Defines a custom highlighter that applies a style to email addresses. The highlights list contains a regex with a named group 'email'. The base_style 'example.' is prefixed to the group name to form the style 'example.email', which is defined in a custom Theme. The highlighter is set on the Console to apply to all printed text.

```python
from rich.console import Console
from rich.highlighter import RegexHighlighter
from rich.theme import Theme

class EmailHighlighter(RegexHighlighter):
    """Apply style to anything that looks like an email."""

    base_style = "example."
    highlights = [r"(?P<email>[\w-]+@([\w-]+\.)+[\w-]+)"]


theme = Theme({"example.email": "bold magenta"})
console = Console(highlighter=EmailHighlighter(), theme=theme)
console.print("Send funds to money@example.org")
```
